**Q1 FY26 (call 2026-04-29):** Revenue $181.5B, +17% y/y (+15% ex-FX); operating income $23.9B; **operating margin 13.1% — highest ever**. AWS grew **28% y/y, fastest in 15 quarters**, +$2B q/q (largest Q4→Q1 step-up ever), $150B run-rate. **AWS backlog $364B** (Jassy, Q1) — and explicitly **excludes the >$100B Anthropic deal** announced subsequently. AI revenue run-rate >$15B; Bedrock customer spend +170% q/q, 125k+ customers, ~80% of the Fortune 100; OpenAI's GPT-5.4 added to Bedrock (5.5 imminent). **Custom silicon >$20B run-rate, +40% q/q; Trainium revenue commitments exceed $225B**; Trainium3 "nearly fully subscribed." Retail unit growth +15% (highest since COVID); grocery now #2 US grocer ($150B+ gross sales '25). **Q1 cash capex $43.2B**; FY26 capex guide ~$200B (Q4'25 call), predominantly AWS.

- **JPM "AI Capex 2.0" (2026-06-16):** corroborates the AWS reacceleration — 1Q cloud +28% on a **$150bn run-rate**, +$2bn q/q (largest 4Q→1Q AWS step-up ever), and a **$364bn backlog that excludes the >$100bn Anthropic expansion**. Custom silicon now central: **>$225bn of Trainium revenue commitments from Anthropic + OpenAI**, with mgmt arguing the program saves "tens of billions in annual capex." Funding math: FY26 capex **~$200bn → ~$300bn FY27E → ~$350bn FY28E**; TTM FCF already collapsed to **~$1bn (from ~$26bn a year ago)**, leaving a cumulative ~$97bn funding gap FY26-28 before the OpenAI/Anthropic facilities. AMZN responded with **~$67bn of bond issuance across four currencies in 1H** plus a new **$17.5bn three-year delayed-draw term loan (Sept-30 commitment expiry)** — ~$85bn of new funding capacity arranged in <6 months without touching equity (against a ~$33bn FY26 gap), which JPM reads as pre-funding the FY27 ramp; ~$143bn cash on hand. Model-lab entanglement: $15bn OpenAI stake + a further $35bn equity commitment letter, plus Anthropic investments that could total ~$33bn and generated ~$17bn of MtM gains in 1Q (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16).
- **OpenAI/Anthropic compute deals on AWS (JPM "AI Capex 2.0" capacity table, 2026-06-16):** OpenAI–AWS = **$38bn 7-yr** (Nov-25, NVIDIA GB200/GB300) **plus a ~$100bn+ / ~2 GW Trainium deal (Feb-26)** starting 2027; Anthropic–AWS = **up to 5 GW / $100bn+ over 10 years** (Trainium + Graviton). Anthropic trains/serves Claude on **>1M Trainium2 chips (Project Rainier)** and has committed to run Claude on Trainium "for the next decade" — JPM's strongest third-party endorsement of ASIC economics; AWS positions Trainium3 UltraServers at **~30% better TCO per FP8 PFLOP vs Nvidia GB300 NVL72**.
- **Logistics/fulfillment buildout — still in investment, not harvest (Jefferies/MWPVL, 2026-04-09):** MWPVL's Mark frames AMZN as past the bottom of the excess-capacity valley and re-entering an industrial-real-estate **investment cycle — square footage added to the global supply chain in 2027 will be ~35% higher than 2026**. ~1/3 of two-year retail capex is US robotics fulfillment centers; new layer of **six mega-hub sortation centers (~$1.5B total, 80–100k packages/hr)** positions AMZN as a full air/ground/LTL package competitor, targeting a **fully Amazon-Logistics-controlled network by ~2030** (unwinding UPS/USPS). ~6.7B AMZN Logistics packages in 2024; grocery $150B+ gross sales 2025 (#2 US grocer) but online-grocery margins skeptical; pharmacy rolling into the 86 sub-same-day centers (1–3hr Rx delivery). Shift to **ownership over leasing** (190 new facilities in pipeline, 118 owned); 1M+ robots built in-house. Key buy-side caution: automation is ~14–15yr payback / near-breakeven — there is **"no boogie man" margin offset** to the AWS investment cycle on the retail side (Jefferies/MWPVL, 2026-04-09). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_09_jeff_mwpvl_9_apr_26.html)
- **Advertising / digital-ad checks (rich, 2026-06-22):** AMZN ad spend is the standout in UBS's Scott Linzer agency checks — **Q2 +4%, Q3 ~7%, Q4 +7.5–8%, FY ~6–7%**, a meaningful step-up across the board; **Amazon DSP is the explicit call-out**, taking budget from The Trade Desk (now at parity/surpassing on data + ROI), with sponsored products "still working" and new Prime Video/streaming inventory (Netflix/Roku/Disney) inside DSP. Tariff refunds to retailer clients are being "pocketed by Mr. CFO," not yet reallocated to ads — a potential 2H incremental lever (UBS "Scott Linzer ad checks", 2026-06-22). UBS Ben Legg corroborates the mix: Amazon ad FY ~27% (sponsored products 65% of spend, softening to ~+16% May vs +21% April — possible agentic-search headwind + maxed ad loads + merchant-margin squeeze) but **video ads +83% y/y and DSP +77% y/y, both accelerating**; Amazon has "declared war" on Trade Desk (20% vs 1% take rate) (UBS Ben Legg, 2026-06-17). 3P-seller channel checks: new fees pressuring sellers — 3.5% fuel surcharge + can no longer use credit cards to pay for ads; Prime Day pulled earlier (June); Rufus sponsored ads live but advertisers have "zero control" / minimal volume; Q1'26 ad budgets strong, no compression (Jefferies 3P-seller expert, 2026-04-09). [Sources](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_22_scott_linzer_ubs_adv_call_22_jun_26.html) · [Ben Legg](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_17_ben_legg_ubs_17_jun_26.html) · [3P seller](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_09_amzn_jeff_9_apr_26.html)
- **Bernstein "Cloud in the Quarter" (2026-06-22):** reiterates **Outperform, TP $315**; calls AMZN the leading infra player, expected to lead hyperscalers in getting compute online thanks to BOM-cost advantages amid tightening/rising-BOM supply. **Trainium ~$225B revenue commitments**; Trainium2 ~30% better price/perf vs comparable GPUs and largely sold out; Trainium3 (shipping early-2026) 30-40% better price/perf than Trn2, nearly fully subscribed; Trainium4 (~18mo out) already largely reserved. **Graviton** advantage in sharper focus amid constrained CPU supply (up to 40% better price/perf vs x86; Meta signed for "tens of millions of Graviton cores"). Chips >$20B ARR, +40% q/q. Op margins seen healthy in **low-to-mid 30%** range; AWS OI $14.2B / ~38% margin (+270bps q/q), with custom-ASIC efficiency a margin offset. Data-gravity (enterprises running AI where data resides) cited as a structural AI-revenue driver (Bernstein "Cloud in the Quarter", 2026-06-22).
- **SemiAnalysis "Anthropic Growth & Bedrock Mix" (2026-05-27) — the Bedrock margin engine, quantified:** the clearest third-party teardown of *why* AWS margins inflected. AWS EBIT margins rose **+213bp Q/Q** — while Oracle, CoreWeave and Azure saw flat-to-declining cloud margins and GCP's rise is "illusory" (see GOOG below) — driven primarily by customer spending growth on **Claude through Bedrock**. The structural edge is the deal mechanic: on Bedrock, **Anthropic is seller-of-record and books the full gross token revenue, while AWS collects BOTH an EC2-like infrastructure fee AND a distribution/rev-share fee** — the latter is what makes TaaS structurally higher-margin than wholesale AI IaaS. Bedrock is a **~$5.5B run-rate business** with **80-90%+ of customers on Anthropic models**, now **37% of AWS AI revenue (up from 9% in 1Q25)** vs AI IaaS still 80%+ of the mix at Azure/GCP; it grew **170% Q/Q in 1Q26** (60% in 4Q25). Punchline: **Bedrock drove ~30% of the Y/Y AWS gross-profit-dollar step-up despite being only ~4% of total AWS revenue**, and SemiAnalysis models Bedrock rising to ~53% of AWS AI revenue in 2Q (adding ~9 points to total AWS revenue growth), with Anthropic Bedrock revenue ~$26M/MW in 1Q26 → ~$42M/MW in 2Q implying Bedrock EBIT margins ~55%. Vertical integration compounds it — Garman: Trainium "now powers >50% of Bedrock token usage"; Graviton4 heads Trn3 nodes; AWS has large CPU/Graviton deals with Anthropic, OpenAI and Meta. AWS also builds more capacity than any CSP in 2025/26/27 and has the **lowest server depreciation (5yr) of all CSPs yet the only one with a true rising-margin trend** (SemiAnalysis "Anthropic Growth & Bedrock Mix", 2026-05-27). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_Bedrock.html)
- **Barclays "AI's S-Curve Is Steepening" (2026-06-01) — AWS margin inflection + capex peak pushed out:** Barclays (Sandler) flags the **AWS operating-margin uptick to 37.7% in 1Q** as evidence of the shift from "bare metal" to **inference-as-a-service** at custom-silicon hyperscalers driving infra-layer margins higher (token efficiency up + revenue-per-token up at the labs). Frames the AI S-curve as steepening on Claude Code/Codex adoption; the **capex peak now pushed beyond 2028 with ~36 GW of next-gen compute** (vs a prior ~2028 peak), and Barclays' hyperscaler-capex aggregate (incl. AWS) goes to **$674B (2026E) → $919B (2027E) → ~$1.16T (2028E)**. AWS is a primary inference hyperscaler for the labs — modeled to challenge Azure for the OpenAI lead by '28 — with AWS contracts of **$38B (2025-32) + $100B (2026-33) for OpenAI** in Barclays' schedule. Reiterates **OW/Positive, PT $330** (Barclays, 2026-06-01). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Barclays_U_S_Internet_Semiconductors_AI_s_S_Curve_Is_Steepening.html)
- **Bernstein Gen-AI Handbook + BofA "AI 2030" — TAM/framework context (historical):** Bernstein's 247-page Handbook (2026-06-22) groups AWS among the four hyperscalers (MSFT/AMZN/GOOG/ORCL) selling IaaS-PaaS and argues that AI accelerates cloud migration of critical workloads through 2030, growing the IaaS-PaaS TAM — Amazon a named beneficiary of incremental database/compute usage (Bernstein "Scaling Intelligence", 2026-06-22, thematic). BofA's older "AI 2030" note (Vivek Arya, 2026-05-13) sizes the AI-accelerator TAM at **~$1.2Tn by CY30** on increased hyperscaler custom-ASIC shipments, naming **AWS Trainium** alongside Google TPU as the diversification driver — historical TAM color consistent with, but superseded in granularity by, the SemiAnalysis Trainium3 work and JPM's >$225B Trainium-commitment datapoint above.
- **Barron's "Top CEOs of 2026" — Jassy profile (Barron's/Nate Wolf, 2026-06-22):** AWS projected at **$168B revenue in 2026, up 30% y/y**; Amazon logged **$215B of capex in the past two years** following AWS's 2021 Meta cloud deal. Jassy on the April earnings call, cited by Barron's: "We continue to be confident in the long-term capex investments we're making" (Barron's, 2026-06-22). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Barrons_22_June_2026.html)
- **AWS fee hike + acceleration drivers (BofA · Justin Post, 2026-06-30):** AWS **raising fees +20% on select GPU workloads effective July 1**, expected to add **1-2pt to 2H26 AWS growth**; acceleration further driven by **OpenAI models added to Bedrock**, capacity ramp for the **$38bn OpenAI commitment**, and early ramp of the **$100bn Anthropic commitment** (BofA · Justin Post, 2026-06-30).
- **Internet-capex/capacity/monetization deep-dive — Amazon the "capacity leader," Buy reiterated (BofA "Raising Internet capex ests", 2026-07-07):** BofA (Justin Post) raises sector capex estimates and translates capex into GW capacity and revenue-per-GW. For **AWS (ex-retail): capex $159bn 2026 (unchanged) / $230bn 2027 (vs $196bn prior)**. Amazon **adds the most capacity of the Big-3 — ~15GW over 2026-27 (vs Google +9GW, Meta +~6GW)** — and at the **lowest cost per incremental GW: ~$25bn/GW in 2026 (vs Google $37bn, Meta $45bn)**, reflecting AWS's scale, internal chips (Trainium/Graviton), and a more balanced core-cloud (vs AI) workload mix. **AWS revenue per Cloud GW ~$10.6bn (2026)** (assuming 70% of AWS capacity is Cloud; 15.8GW of the 22.6GW base is Cloud). In the valuation cut, BofA backs out core retail/ads and finds an **implied Street value of ~$59bn per AWS GW** (2027 capacity, 70% for AI/Cloud) — a discount to Google's ~$110bn/GW, which BofA attributes to lower Cloud rev/GW yield and less perceived AI/LLM differentiation. Recent Anthropic & Google–SpaceX capacity deals at **>$40bn/GW (up to ~$50bn/GW for specialized AI capacity)** — well above the $10-17bn/GW BofA models for AWS/Google Cloud — signal upside to Cloud-revenue estimates. **Reiterate Buy, PO $310.** Biggest 2Q concern: higher capex / lower FCF overshadowing strong revenue (BofA "Raising Internet capex ests", 2026-07-07). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bofa_on_Capex.html)
- **Trainium3 unit demand estimates raised (UBS · Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01):** UBS lifts Trainium3 unit demand estimates to **1.8mn units (2026E) / 2.8mn units (2027E)**, up from a prior **1.7mn/2.3mn** (UBS · Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01).
- **GS Q2'26 earnings preview — Buy, PT raised $325→$335; the four key debates framed (GS "Amazon Q2'26 Earnings Preview", Eric Sheridan, 2026-07-07):** GS reiterates **Buy and raises its 12-mo PT to $335 (from $325)**, price $245.98 (~36% upside), and shifts to a **SOTP methodology** (16x EV/GAAP-EBIT NA + 1.3x EV/Sales Int'l + **29x EV/GAAP-EBIT AWS, up from 28.5x**). GS raises FY26-28 estimates: **revenue $827.6B/$952.9B/$1,081.0B** (FY27 up from $946.6B); FY27/28 EPS trimmed slightly to $10.15/$12.84 on higher D&A. The note frames the intra-quarter stock volatility (downward trend) around four debates: **(1) Consumer health** — GS reads Prime Day (sales +9.5% YoY to ~$26.4B per Adobe; ~42% of orders <$20, ~$23 avg item; Numerator) as a **stable-but-price-sensitive consumer** trading down into staples/essentials, with purchasing strength concentrated in higher-income households. **(2) AI capex & ROIC** — GS **raises 2027-2030 AWS capex** on demand/supply imbalance + input-cost inflation (DC construction, 3rd-party chips, memory), now modeling **~$827B cumulative capex 2026-28** and **AWS ~$200-250B of 2027 capex** (cost to build 1 GW = **$50-60B**: ~$15B physical + $35-45B IT hardware); capex-to-revenue lag 0.5-2.0 yrs; FCFE goes negative in 2027 (~$(41)B) for the first time — funded via debt (avg cost of debt 3.17%→4.45%), no equity needed. **(3) AWS growth** — GS raises AWS revenue: **~33% YoY 2026, ~35% 2027, ~29% 4-yr CAGR '25-'29**; +32% Q2'26E; backlog-conversion framework implies ~10-20% upside to current AWS numbers; a potential **20% EC2 price hike in 2H26 = ~$2-4B net rev tailwind** (EC2 ~50%+ of AWS). **(4) Trainium/custom silicon** — GS argues Trainium positions AMZN well and is a **forward operating-margin tailwind**: Trainium2 "largely sold out," Trainium3 "nearly fully subscribed," annual chip run-rate **$20B** (**$50B if sold to 3Ps**), Trainium3 **+40% price/perf vs Trainium2** (which was +30-40% vs comparable GPUs); doesn't expect Trainium to displace NVDA GPUs for training but sees inference-cost focus + supply constraints as supportive. Also: **Rufus rebranded to Alexa+ ($12B annualized incremental sales, assisted 300M customers)**; 451 Research puts AMZN at **~6.85 GW of DC capacity across ~200 facilities (2025), +3.9 GW added in 2025 (doubled 2022 footprint), targeting a doubling of 2025 capacity by 2027**. Key risks: eCommerce/Cloud competition, margin headwinds from investment, regulatory, macro. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Q226_Earnings_Preview__Addressing_Key_Operating_Themes_Investor_Debates.html)
- **Amazon Kuiper / Anthropic–AWS read-throughs from the JPM SpaceX initiation (JPM "SpaceX — The Next Frontier", 2026-07-07):** JPM's SpaceX note frames **Amazon LEO (Kuiper)** as a D2D/broadband competitor to Starlink — FCC-authorized for **~7,725 satellites but <400 launched to date**, gated by slow non-SpaceX launch cadence (ULA Vulcan / Blue Origin New Glenn) — i.e. Kuiper's ramp is launch-supply-constrained relative to Starlink's 9,600+ constellation. Separately the note's neocloud analysis reiterates **Anthropic renting external compute at a blended ~$29/W** (Hopper + Blackwell) from SpaceX's Colossus — context for the AWS–Anthropic multi-silicon relationship (Anthropic's willingness to pay up across clouds is a compute-scarcity signal). AWS Trainium sits in JPM's AI-DC power model as one of the custom-silicon platforms; see [SPCX.md](SPCX.md) for the full initiation and [ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md).
- **2027 IT-capacity build vs peers (Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak et al, 2026-07-01):** MS estimates AMZN adds **~5GW of IT capacity in 2027**, vs META's ~3.5GW and GOOGL's ~9GW (Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak et al, 2026-07-01).
- **Broadcom-side reads (light) — direct networking sales + Trainium 4 SOIC (JPMorgan · Harlan Sur "Hock Tan @ JPM", 2026-07-07; JPMorgan · Gokul "TSMC 2Q26 preview", 2026-07-08):** two light supplier-side datapoints. (1) On the Hock Tan call, Hock said **Amazon "claims they can do it all themselves" on networking and commoditizes it, so Broadcom sells direct to Amazon** (no white-box/NOS middleman) — and referenced **ByteDance-style HBM consignment as a bad idea he won't do** (HBM integral to the XPU). (2) Gokul's TSMC preview flags **AWS Trainium 4 moving to TSMC SOIC**, with the merchant Vera/Venice CPUs' CoWoS **heavily dependent on OSATs (ASE/Amkor)** — a packaging-roadmap read on the Trainium 4 (Alchip-backend) ramp already on the page. (JPMorgan · Harlan Sur "Hock Tan @ JPM", 2026-07-07; JPMorgan · Gokul "TSMC 2Q26 preview", 2026-07-08)
- **Project Houdini + "SAMDC" — AWS's construction advantage, quantified (SemiAnalysis "The Wild Wild West Of LEGO Datacenters", 2026-07-29):** in a 75-page modular-construction deep dive, **AWS "shows the fastest scaling buildout currently, adding almost 3.9 GW of capacity to the end of 2025,"** and is the reference case for **operator-led modularization** — engineering the spec itself, procuring owner-furnished gear, and handing an integrator only the assembly (a model "effectively confined to the largest hyperscalers" because it means carrying all the cost, inventory and lead-time risk without vendor allocation leverage). The hard numbers:
  - **Project Houdini** re-cuts AWS's standard data-hall map into a **factory-built skid ~45 feet long, transportable on low double-drop trailers engineered to stay under bridges** — squarely a *factory-built white-space* product. It "**cuts deployment from as much as 15 weeks to roughly 2–3 weeks and can eliminate more than 50,000 on-site electrician hours per module**," with skids built in **Houston, Salt Lake City and Topeka**, early deployments in **Texas and South Bend**, and a target of **~25 weeks from construction start to the first server room**.
  - **Governance is the differentiated bit:** instead of relying only on site-level engineering sign-off, Houdini's factory process "**requires separate validation of the design and the physical build**," with **Cupertino Electric** ([PWR](PWR.md)) the main partner responsible for the latter and the design partner on the spec side.
  - **Beyond white space, the shell is changing too:** "**AWS is now rolling out at very large scale their own modular design codenamed 'SAMDC'**" — narrower, more repeatable structures organised around the systems installed inside, giving "**less building per MW, shorter structural spans, and fewer interfaces for field crews to assemble.**"
  - **Why it shows up in the model, not just the press release:** SemiAnalysis's bottom-up on a 50 MW liquid-cooled hall puts full modular at **~36% (7-9 months) faster and ~8% cheaper all-in (~$13.5mn vs ~$14.6mn/MW)**, and values an earlier month to an owner-operator at **~$500k/MW** (GPU depreciation ~$30mn/MW over five years) — ~**$200mn undiscounted, ~$4mn/MW, on an ~8-month lead**. For a hyperscaler carrying its own GPUs, that is the cleanest link from construction method to AWS capex efficiency and time-to-revenue. Governing caveat: "**go-live is the latest of three dates, building ready, power available, and GPUs delivered.**" (SemiAnalysis "The Wild Wild West Of LEGO Datacenters", 2026-07-29)
- **Graviton5 and the AWS custom-CPU strategy (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09) — HEAVY:** AWS was the first hyperscaler to deploy its own cloud CPUs, via the Annapurna Labs acquisition plus ARM Neoverse CSS reference designs, letting it go direct-to-TSMC/OSAT rather than buying Intel Xeons (p.45). Generational lineage (AWS's own chart, p.46): Graviton1 (2018, 16 cores, 5B transistors) → Graviton2 (2019, 64 cores, 30B) → Graviton3 (2021, 64 cores, 55B) → Graviton4 (2023, 96 cores, 73B) → **Graviton5 (2025, 192 cores, 172B transistors)**. Graviton3 was the first move to ARM Neoverse V1 (2x FP perf vs N1), a 10x7 core mesh (65 cores printed/64 enabled), 4 DDR5+2 PCIe5 I/O chiplets around a central compute die on TSMC N5 with Intel EMIB packaging — "one of the first datacenter CPUs to deploy DDR5 and PCIe5, a full year ahead of AMD and Intel" (p.45-46). Graviton4 moved to Neoverse V2, +50% cores/memory-channels (96 cores, 12-channel), 30-45% speedups, PCIe5 lanes tripled 32→96, added dual-socket support (p.46). **Graviton5** (in preview since December 2025) doubles cores to 192 on Neoverse V3, 172B transistors on TSMC 3nm; L2 stays at 2MB/core but shared L3 jumps from a "paltry" 36MB (Graviton4) to 192MB — extra cache buffers the fact memory bandwidth only rose 57% (12-channel DDR5-8800) despite cores doubling (p.46-47). Architecture evolved to a chiplet design where 2 cores now share one mesh stop (8x12 mesh); AWS didn't disclose die/package configs this round but confirmed "a novel packaging strategy" (p.47). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/CPU_semianalysis_feb_26.html)
- **JPM US Internet 2Q26 Preview — reiterates OW, Dec-26 PT $330, top pick #2 (JPM · Anmuth "US Internet 2Q26 Preview", 2026-07-14):** JPMe **2Q26 net sales $198.6B (guide $194-199B), AWS +32.0% (buyside +34-35%)**; FY26/27/28 revenue **$824.0B / $934.2B / $1,060.6B**, **AWS FY26 +32.4%**, GAAP EPS **$9.00 / $10.38 / $12.95**; op income 2Q26E **$23.9B (guide $20-24B; buyside $24-25B)**, FY26 $105.5B. Capex **$199.6B FY26 (JPMe $204.2B; mgmt ~$200B; buyside $200-210B) → $290.7B FY27**; FCF **–$34.5B FY26 → –$35.7B FY27 → +$1.9B FY28** (JPM shows FCF bottoming 2026-27). Key debates: AWS accel to mid-30s (40% exit?), will AMZN raise the 2026 capex guide + the ROIC of heavy AI spend, Trainium/chip capacity + utilization, consumer/Prime Day. Graviton5 GA; Project Leo capitalization expected 4Q. Investor sentiment "Positive." [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/JPM_JPM_ANMUTH___US_Inte_2026-07-14_5366760.html)
- **JPM 2Q preview call + MS "$1.4T" podcast — the AWS setup into the print (JPM · Anmuth 2Q preview call, 2026-07-14; Morgan Stanley · Nowak "Hyperscale capex to $1.4T", 2026-07-14):** Anmuth reads AMZN as more crowded/loved than Google by a smidge but "the stock doesn't work" despite AWS ~35% — the unlock is **operating-profit beats** (3Q OI guide framed ~$21.5-26.5B); 2026 capex ~$200B (little change, focus is 2027, buyside **$275-400B, most 325-350B**), FCF **burning ~$35B both '26 and '27**, AWS **capacity set to double by end-2027**; Trainium doubt "quieted a lot" (Anthropic adoption, Trn3→Trn4), Graviton underestimated for inference; Prime Day Q3→Q2 shift a modeling wrinkle. Nowak's podcast quantifies the compute: **AMZN ~14 GW (2025) → 36 GW by 2028** (+7 GW this year, +9 next, 2H-weighted), **AWS +35% '26 / +40% '27** on a deliberately conservative **$7-9 incremental revenue per watt (lowest of the group)** → upward bias; **AWS backlog +$110B q/q in 2Q** (to ~$475B, per the companion 07-12 MS note on-page), incremental margins high-30s/40. [JPM call](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_14_jpm_preview.html) · [MS podcast](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_14_brian_nowak_on_hyperscale_capex_raise_to_1_4t_new_.html)
- **Archera expert call — the "capacity wars" tighten AWS's hand; Trainium serves Sonnet but not the biggest models (Archera expert call, 2026-07-13):** an ex-AWS operator's channel view — as of start-Q2, **just to get A100 access on AWS you now need a ≥1-year commitment** (Amazon floated a "1-yr commit to 100 A100s" to a customer who then went to neoclouds), and Amazon is forcing shorter commitments to protect margin. Amazon's late-2025 rule that **any customer with a >$5M annual AWS commitment must go direct** (no partner) is squeezing the 5-7% partner margins. On silicon: **Sonnet appears servable off Trainium2 with "no capacity issues," but Opus 4.28 / Fable show capacity issues — so Amazon is "still all on Nvidia GPUs," making AWS token supply less fungible than Google's TPU-served Vertex.** Structural cost edge on the CPU side: **>50% of AWS's data-center CPUs have been Graviton for two years**, giving Amazon the most BOM headroom of the hyperscalers to absorb the 2026 RAM-price spike (vs Azure Intel/AMD-only, Google's under-deployed Axion). Bedrock cited as a US-vendor middleware wrapper enabling enterprise adoption of open-source (incl. Chinese) models. **Net-new from the raw Jefferies transcript ("Aran K", 2026-07-13):** unlike Google/Azure — which are "fairly forward-footed" on leasing third-party capacity to fill the demand gap for their customers — **Amazon is "less prone" to lease external capacity** (for competitive-advantage reasons); and both AWS and Google are **prioritizing inference capacity over accelerating Rubin deployment** (both see inference as the bigger near-term opportunity, vs neoclouds racing to deploy Rubin first). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_13_archera_13_jul_26.html)
- **Archera/JEF cloud call — CSP terms tightening in the clouds' favor (@SouthernValue95, 2026-07-14):** a second read of the Jefferies-hosted Archera call corroborating the "capacity wars" bullet above — customers trying to renew 3-yr deals are being told they **can only renew 1-yr (smaller discounts = higher price)**; reserving 3-yr capacity now requires **bigger minimum commitments (rising min cluster size) + annual spend cadence**; **GPU and CPU prices going up**; supply > demand, so when clients push back on terms the clouds are **"pushing them to $ORCL or the neoclouds"** (neocloud momentum framed as demand overflow, not share loss). Read as positive for the AI/DC trade incl. AMZN/GOOG, with under-appreciated ROIC upside on existing CSP DC fleets (@SouthernValue95, 2026-07-14).
- **Bedrock multi-model breadth — GPT-5.6 now GA (OpenAI/@gdb, 2026-07-14):** OpenAI's Greg Brockman confirms **GPT-5.6 is now generally available on AWS Bedrock** — incremental proof of the multi-model Bedrock platform thesis (Anthropic + OpenAI + open-source under one AWS-hosted roof), the distribution-endpoint dynamic the sell-side keeps flagging (OpenAI/@gdb, 2026-07-14).
- **AWS network topology "RNG" (Resilient Network Graphs) (@SemiAnalysis_, 2026-07-14):** SemiAnalysis details Amazon's latest DC network topology **unveiled in May — "RNG" (Resilient Network Graphs), built on a random-graph concept rather than the traditional fat-tree** used across the industry — a networking-architecture differentiator underpinning AWS's large-cluster scale-out (relevant to the Trainium/Project Rainier build). (@SemiAnalysis_, 2026-07-14)
- **AWS CoWoS allocation: share slips, wafers still +54% — read the denominator, not the percentage (Fubon "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates", Sherman Shang / Daniel Yang, 2026-07-28):** in Fubon's TSMC CoWoS customer-mix forecast, **AWS runs 14.1% (2023) → 12.4% (2024) → 12.7% (2025) → 10.9% (2026) → 10.4% (2027)** — but the wafer counts behind those shares are **18,200 → 37,220 → 85,650 → 127,500 → 196,500**, i.e. **+49% in 2026 and +54% in 2027**, on a total pool going **675,000 (2025) → 1,155,600 shipped / 1,170,000 capacity (2026) → 1,890,000 (2027)**. **State it plainly: the declining share is a denominator effect, not an allocation cut** — the pool grows +64% in 2027 while AWS grows +54%, so Trainium wafers keep compounding hard even as NVDA holds 50.0% (945,000 wafers), AVGO takes 22.3% (421,800) and MediaTek jumps 1.5% → 7.3% (17,400 → 137,700) on the Google TPU program. AWS remains the **third-largest CoWoS customer** in the book behind NVDA and AVGO, and ahead of AMD (7.0%, 132,300) and MSFT (1.0%, 18,900 — Microsoft entering the mix for the first time in 2026). Useful as an independent supply-side cross-check on the Trainium3/Trainium4 volume marks already on this page (UBS · Sunny Lin: 1.8mn units '26E / 2.8mn '27E) — the wafer trajectory is directionally consistent with that unit ramp. (Fubon, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Our_advanced_packaging_CoWoS_forecast_adjustments_other_up.html)
- **Anthropic-Samsung foundry read-through (@jukan05, 2026-07-14):** Korean media reports **Samsung's foundry has secured Anthropic as a customer to manufacture Anthropic's custom AI chips** — a watch item for the AWS-Trainium franchise, since Anthropic (the marquee >$100B Trainium customer / Project Rainier) pursuing its own custom silicon is a potential longer-term diversification away from single-sourcing on Trainium. Unverified single-source report; flagged as a read-through, not a confirmed shift (@jukan05, 2026-07-14).

**Q2'26 guide (Q1 call):** net sales $194–199B; OI $20–24B. Headwinds flagged: ~$1B y/y LEO cost step-up and higher transport/fuel costs (partly offset by FBA surcharge); memory/component costs "skyrocketed." The Q2 top line is helped by **Prime Day moving into 2Q for the first time since 2021** — the 4-day event (June 23–26, 23 countries) shifts an estimated **~$7B–$8B of incremental global revenue into 2Q26, already reflected in the $194–199B guide** (JPM est. ~6% y/y growth/day in 3P and ~7% in 1P, excluding ad/sub upside) (JPM/Anmuth — Prime deep-dive, 2026-06-23). Bernstein (Mark Shmulik, 2026-04-30) calls the OI guide "very conservative" and reads the rev guide as implying continued AWS acceleration into "low-to-mid 30s." Jassy framed AI demand as **"barbelled"** (Q4'25 call) — AI labs + runaway apps on one end, enterprise productivity/cost-avoidance on the other — with the durable middle (enterprise production workloads + net-new AI-native apps) still to come.

## Debate / thesis
- **Post-Q2'26: the ROI debate got an answer, the FCF debate got worse (2026-07-30).** The two halves of the print pull in opposite directions and both are now on the page permanently. **For the bulls:** AWS +36.7% is the fastest in 18 quarters and *accelerating* off a $150B base, the backlog went $364B → **$496B** in one quarter, AWS margin *expanded* to 39.4% while capex ripped, and management put hard numbers on payback for the first time — **<3 years to break even on servers/networking, ≥5-6 year useful life, ≥5-year contracted terms on most AI capacity, and 5-6 server generations off a single 30+ year shell**. That is the specific rebuttal to the "circular / unprovable ROI" bear case that drove the July de-rating, and it is why the AI-supply-chain complex (ALAB/MRVL/CRDO) traded up on someone else's print (MS Wigg, 2026-07-30). **For the bears:** **FCF was −$9B against consensus −$1B**, and FY26 capex went **~$200B → ~$220B with the increment attributed to memory-cost inflation** — Vital Knowledge's framing is the cleanest: "the only two numbers that anyone will talk about are 37% and 39.4% … but people should also talk about −$9B, as this speaks to the cash pressures facing hyperscalers" (Vital Knowledge, 2026-07-30). Note what that $20B capex increment actually is: **not more compute, just a higher price for the same compute.** It is the same memory squeeze that took AAPL's underlying gross margin to ~46.5% the same evening, arriving on the hyperscaler side as capex dollars rather than COGS. **Our read:** the ROI disclosure is the more durable of the two facts, because it is structural (asset-life arithmetic) while the FCF drag is timing (spend precedes monetization by ~2 years, by management's own description). The thing to underwrite is not the FCF print but whether the ~40% AWS exit rate the buy side now models (UBS Dwyer, 2026-07-30) is supply-constrained — management said demand exceeds supply through 2027, which caps upside as much as it de-risks it.
- **Contrast with MSFT/META the night before is the real signal (2026-07-30).** Three-for-three on cloud beats (MS Wigg): MSFT Azure +43% → guided +45%, GOOGL Cloud +82%, AWS +36.7%. But the *capex* messages diverged sharply — MSFT held CY26 capex at ~$190B (the headline drop to ~$175B was a lease-classification change, not a spend cut), META narrowed to $130-145B and raised only the low end, while **AMZN raised outright to ~$220B**. The market rewarded MSFT (+9-15%) and AMZN (+8-10%) and punished META (-8-10%), which tells you the discriminator is not the level of spend but whether accelerating cloud revenue is visibly arriving alongside it. AMZN and MSFT have that; META is still selling a promise (JEF Wong "Reading the Tech Leaves", 2026-07-30; Bernstein Seidman, 2026-07-30).
- **Bull:** AWS re-acceleration is the core long. AI demand "exploding," dragging core compute with it (post-training, RL, agentic tool-use) — Jassy, Q1'26. Custom-silicon edge is the differentiator: Trainium 30–40% better price/perf vs comparable GPUs, lets AMZN "save billions on capex and extract points of margin leverage" (Shmulik, Bernstein 2026-04-30) — the only hyperscaler to **hold capex flat** while peers raised on input-cost inflation, signaling supply-chain discipline. Backlog breadth improving: ~$20B of the sequential step-up is **non-frontier-lab**, the highest on record (Shmulik). Jefferies named AMZN a **top 2H26 pick** (Brent Thill, briefing 2026-06-06); at the Jefferies internet conf (2026-06-01) AWS/Bedrock cited as the structural winner as enterprises push net-new app builds to the cloud. MS (Brian Nowak) flags SpaceX $50/watt compute deal as ~16% upside to AWS '27 rev if monetization reaches $25/watt (briefing 2026-06-18). Into 1Q'26, JPM (Anmuth) framed AMZN as the "AI laggard→winner" transition with the OpenAI deal adding ~$100B to backlog at the print and AI ARR >$15B / chips >$20B (JPM internet preview, 2026-04-15); BofA (Fenske spec-sales) called AWS "my favorite business," with Jassy's redemption arc (OpenAI $50B + Anthropic = "two AI winners solidified," Trainium3/4 sold out, ~$50B if Trainium were an external chip program vs Broadcom's ~$100B AI-semi goal) (BofA, 2026-04-16). Fresh buyside corroboration of the reaccel long: BofA's informal buyside bogey poll now has AWS **reaccelerating from +34.3% Q2 to +35.7% Q3** (reported growth) — slightly above GS's ~33%/~35% '26/'27 — i.e. the AWS-reacceleration long is now the buyside base case, not just a sell-side call (BofA · Brian Fenske (buyside bogey poll), 2026-07-12). [JPM](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_15_jpm_internet_preview_15_apr_26.html) · [BofA](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_16_fenske_16_apr_26.html)
- **AWS margin durability + 1Q'26 print read-throughs (multiple recaps, 2026-04-30):** post-print callbacks corroborated the AWS-acceleration long. AWS grew 28% with **1Q AWS margins better than expected and "no one-timers"** (mgmt reframed the historical "high-20s to low-30s" range as effectively just "low-30s" — no longer expecting the low end); Trainium saves AWS **"several hundred bps" of margin vs renting Nvidia GPUs** (first hard quantification); **Bedrock model-access revenue is booked NET (not gross)** — margin/quality positive (expert recap, 2026-04-30). Citi adds AWS margins are lower now only because it's a "newer-stage, more capital-intensive" business and should recover toward prior levels at scale (Citi C1Q26 recap, 2026-04-30). On capex, AMZN said supply-chain/memory inflation was anticipated and FY26 $200B unchanged, but the expert reads a **~+20%/unit cost impact landing in 2027 (~$235B vs $200B same-units)** (expert recap, 2026-04-30). BofA preview math: ~$150B of the $200B FY26 capex is AWS-driven (~100% of AWS revenue), implying a build toward ~$400B AWS revenue; AI-deal margins are lower (~10–20%, hard to pin) and a near-term Q2 margin risk as Anthropic ramps (BofA/Post preview, 2026-04-20). [Sources](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_30_hyper_wrc_recap_30_apr_2026.html) · [Citi recap](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_30_msft_wfc_c1q26_recap_30_apr_26.html) · [BofA preview](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_20_fenske_20_apr_26.html)
- **AWS as a frontier-model distribution endpoint (UBS Karl Keirstead, 2026-06-17):** UBS's "hyperscaler bull case" argues the AWS/GCP growth accelerations can't be explained by per-hour accelerator rental alone — the hyperscalers are increasingly the **distribution endpoint** for the frontier labs (enterprises rolling out Claude Code report "no commercial relationship with Anthropic — we procure it through AWS/Azure"). Lower gross margin, but a powerful upsell and a structural growth accelerant; UBS is an advocate but flags investor mood on AMZN/MSFT/ORCL "more mixed than I'd have thought" despite the revenue beats (UBS "tokenmaxxing", 2026-06-17). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_18_karl_ubs_18_jun_26.html)
- **Trainium economics / the silicon long (SemiAnalysis "AWS Trainium3 Deep Dive," 2025-12-04):** Most detailed third-party validation of the custom-silicon thesis. Trainium3 (GA + Trainium4 announced at re:Invent) is a "step-function" upgrade — N3P node (from N5), 144GB HBM3E 12-hi (Hynix/Micron, up from sub-par Samsung on Trn2), +70% memory bandwidth, doubled MXFP8 FLOPs + new MXFP4 support, ~1,000W TDP. New all-to-all **switched** scale-up fabric: NL32x2 air-cooled ("Teton3 PDS") is the volume SKU in '26; NL72x2 liquid ("Teton3 MAX") is AWS's answer to Nvidia's GB200 NVL72 — AWS is the **first non-Nvidia vendor to ship a switched scale-up rack** (ahead of AMD MI450X). Core economics: Trainium gives up marketed FP8/FP4 density vs Nvidia/AMD, but systems are "dramatically cheaper" because AWS **avoids the margin stacking embedded in Nvidia servers** → materially lower upfront cluster capex per logical chip; lower operating TCO driven mostly by chip TDP (Trn2 ~500W, Trn3 ~1,000W vs ~1,200W GB200 / 1,400W GB300). Caveat: no native FP4 support → higher TCO per marketed FP4 FLOP than Nvidia. AWS is open-sourcing its software stack (PyTorch backend, NKI compiler, then XLA/JAX) to build a CUDA-style ecosystem; LNC=8 (the mode mainstream researchers want) not supported until mid-2026. AWS's air-cooling-first datacenter design (vs peers' DLC pivots) is a time-to-market + fungibility advantage, benefiting Project Rainier ramp. Bottom line: a credible NVDA challenger on perf-per-TCO, alongside Google TPUv7 and AMD MI450X — "Nvidia stays King as long as Jensen keeps accelerating." Supply-chain read-through: Marvell **lost the Trainium3 socket to Alchip** (poor Trn2 execution); Alchip leads Trn4 backend (UALink + NVLink-Fusion tracks). UBS (2026 overview) sees MRVL retaining only "some revenue for Trainium 3" via the Celestial AI acquisition — i.e. MRVL is no longer the lead designer.
- **SCORECARD / BACKFILL — the Sept-2025 "AWS AI Resurgence" call and its 2027 tail risk (SemiAnalysis "Amazon's AI Resurgence: AWS & Anthropic's Multi-Gigawatt Trainium Expansion", Ontiveros/Patel/Kourabi/Xie, 2025-09-03 — ingested 2026-07-24, ~11 months old; HISTORICAL structural context, supersedes no 2026 figure on this page):** worth keeping on file as a **scorecard document**. At the exact moment the market was marking Amazon as the YTD laggard among the four AI titans, SemiAnalysis made an explicit out-of-consensus call — an **"AWS AI Resurgence," forecasting acceleration "beyond 20% year-over-year growth by the end of 2025"** — against the consensus "cloud crisis" framing it had itself authored in March-2023. Frame: **AWS ≈ 60% of Amazon group profits**, dominant in classic cloud but losing the GPU/XPU-cloud era to Azure (on OpenAI) and a narrowing Google gap (on TPU). **How the call scored:** the management-commentary table below shows AWS +17.5% (Q2'25) → **+20.2% (Q3'25)** → +24% (Q4'25) → +28% (Q1'26) — the >20% threshold was cleared on the Q3'25 print and the acceleration ran well past it, so the directional call played out on this page's own numbers. The physical forecast also landed: SemiAnalysis counted **>1.3GW of IT capacity across three AWS campuses in final construction dedicated solely to Anthropic training, with just under 1 million Trainium2 in the largest campus** ("AWS is building datacenters faster than it ever has in its entire history"), which reads directly onto JPM's later confirmation of **>1M Trainium2 (Project Rainier)** and JPM's 07-07 sizing of New Carlisle at **~910MW**. At the time SemiAnalysis judged those datacenters were generating **no meaningful revenue yet** (Trainium assembly-phase yield issues). **Diagnosis of the GenAI underperformance (still the live structural weakness):** the custom **EFA** backend fabric lags InfiniBand, Spectrum-X and RoCEv2 (Cisco/Arista/Juniper) on both raw performance *and* user experience (EFAv4 improving but still behind), and AWS's custom networking slows its time-to-market on Nvidia systems. **Trainium2 vs GB200, as of Sep-2025:** GB200 had a **3.85x FP16 FLOPs advantage (2,500 vs 667 TFLOP/s/chip)** and **2.75x memory bandwidth (8,000 vs 2,900 GB/s)**; scale-up aggregate bandwidth **576 TB/s (NVL72) vs 186 TB/s (Teton2-PD-Ultra-3L) = 3.1x** — but Trainium2 was **highly competitive on TCO per million tokens and TCO per TB/s of memory bandwidth**, precisely the axis Anthropic's memory-bandwidth-bound (rather than FLOPs-bound) RL roadmap runs on. Roadmap called at the time: Teton PD / Teton PD Ultra in the field → **Teton PDS and Teton Max in volume in 2026**, introducing an all-to-all scale-up network, **NeuronLinkv3** (4 switch trays mid-rack, 16 compute trays above and below) — i.e. Trainium's architecture explicitly converging toward NVLink/NVL72 (the direction the 2025-12-04 Trainium3 deep-dive above then confirmed). **The 2027 tail risk — the real alpha in the note, and still unresolved:** Trainium shipments match SemiAnalysis's forecast Anthropic IT capacity in 2025–26, but **there is a gap in 2027** — "Anthropic will be essentially the sole customer for Trainium2+ in 2025 and 2026 but isn't large enough to absorb the volumes AWS currently aims to produce in 2027," with **no sizeable external Trainium customer found** (the Trainium1/Inferentia2 precedent), so the risk is "a more visible **degradation of AWS' ROIC profile**." Mark it against what has since partly answered it on this page — OpenAI's **~$100B+ / ~2 GW Trainium deal (Feb-26) starting 2027**, >$225B of total Trainium commitments, Jassy's "good chance" of selling Trainium racks externally — but the ROIC question itself is the same one GS/JPM/Redburn are still litigating in 2026. **Bedrock, as of Sep-2025 (an 11-month turnaround, not a current criticism):** rate limits were "a huge blocker for production deployments" — new accounts got **2 RPM for Claude Opus against an advertised 50**, vs Anthropic direct at 50 RPM and **4,000 RPM at Tier 4** — such that "deploying Claude Code using the Bedrock API can cause Claude Code to crash"; and the **GPT-OSS deployment was botched** (the system prompt the model saw was mutated in "random and incoherent ways"), scoring **13% lower than most other inference endpoints** per Artificial Analysis, with SemiAnalysis concluding "the Bedrock team is still struggling to independently deploying a model at full capability." Read against the 2026-05-27 SemiAnalysis note above — which credits Bedrock with driving the entire AWS margin inflection — this is the single sharpest measure of how far the platform moved in under a year. **Internal models:** Nova and Titan "perform poorly" across the board and have seen "little to no updates" since release; Amazon integrates **Claude, not Nova**, into its own products (Alexa+), and "Amazon does not possess the required talent to produce a strong model" — the structural reason the *Nova-as-a-hedge* idea floated in the 06-30 R&Co repricing story is a weak hedge. Supply-chain read-through on the design partners (Marvell/Alchip/Broadcom, HBM procured direct) is carried on [MRVL.md](MRVL.md) and [custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2025_09_03_ENC_Amazons_AI_Resurgence_AWS_Anthropics_Multi-Gigawatt_Trainium_Expansion_email.html)
- **SCORECARD / BACKFILL — FUNDA's Oct-2025 AWS preview: a TRN order-book number worth calibrating against today's production marks, plus the deployment-lag diagnosis (FUNDA "Preview|AWS 3Q25: AWS Acceleration Ahead, but TRN3 Remains the Key", 2025-10-28 — ingested 2026-08-06, ~9 MONTHS OLD; HISTORICAL/SCORECARD context, supersedes NOTHING on this page and sits outside the intra-quarter window by three quarters, filed exactly as the 2025-09-03 SemiAnalysis note above):** ⚠️ **Source hygiene first — this is a print-to-PDF with the RIGHT MARGIN CUT and several sentences end mid-word. Only complete fragments are quoted below; everything else is paraphrased, and no missing word has been guessed.**
  - **⭐ THE SCORECARD ITEM — a Trainium ORDER BOOK from Oct-2025, and it is a calibration datapoint, not a revision.** FUNDA's supply-chain check said that for **2026 roughly 2.2-2.6M TRN units were on order, "with CoWoS corresponding to 120-160K, essentially all TRN3,"** and that **TRN3 orders increased that month while TRN2.5 was significantly cut.** ⚠️ **BASES, and they are three different things: FUNDA's figure is an ORDER BOOK (Oct-2025, forward-looking); the page's Fubon mark is total Trainium chip output of ~1.6-1.7mn (2026) → ~3mn (2027) on a PRODUCTION basis (2026-08-03); UBS/Sunny Lin's is Trainium3 unit DEMAND of 1.8mn (2026E) / 2.8mn (2027E) (2026-07-01). NEITHER EXISTING MARK IS EDITED OR REPLACED.** What the comparison buys is a read on **how much a nine-month-old order book overstates eventual output: 2.2mn vs Fubon's 1.7mn is +29%, and on midpoints 2.4mn vs 1.65mn is +45% (vs UBS's demand mark, 2.4mn vs 1.8mn = +33%)** — i.e. the order book ran roughly **30-45% hot** against what the chain now expects to build. **The CoWoS leg scores far better than the unit leg, and that is the useful asymmetry:** FUNDA's 120-160K CoWoS wafers lands essentially on top of Fubon's later **127,500 AWS CoWoS wafers for 2026** (2026-07-28) — ⚠️ different scopes again (FUNDA's is the TRN3-attributable slice, Fubon's is all of AWS), but close enough to suggest the **packaging** book was directionally right while the **chip-unit** book was inflated. Read for the book: when AWS unit forecasts circulate a year ahead, discount the units and trust the substrate.
  - **FUNDA's own model discipline, worth noting because few publishers do it:** they publish preview accuracy — the **1Q25 preview was "highly accurate," and they "overestimated 2Q25 by 0.5%,"** attributed to rapid price cuts driven by **low Trainium2 utilisation**, with the check that **top-tier customers "may already be leasing Trainum2 [Trainium2] at $0.4-0.5 per hour."** They ran a **neutral ~40% TRN2 utilisation assumption** in the model at the time.
  - **The Trainium2 capability picture as of Oct-2025 (the earliest datapoint in a series this page already carries):** TRN2 essentially could not handle training and was expected to be used mainly for **inference and reinforcement learning**; **"Anthropic heavily uses TPUs for inference, crowding out TRN2/future TRN3 demand"**; only **3.5 Haiku had successfully completed training on TRN2** at that point, with Opus 3.5 and Sonnet 3.5 both failing; and an AI-lab technical expert: **"TRN's software stack is inferior to TPU."** Track that against the page's later marks — the Archera call (2026-07-13: Sonnet servable on TRN2 with no capacity issues, Opus/Fable not) and Garman's "Trainium now powers >50% of Bedrock token usage" (SemiAnalysis 2026-05-27) — as the trajectory of the same question, not as competing claims.
  - **The AWS-specific deployment-lag diagnosis, which is the cleanest early statement of the weakness this page later prices via EFA:** AWS committed to a **unified elastic fabric and unified cooling spanning both GPU and Trainium**, so Blackwell clusters were heavily customised and **"these deployments require months of debugging, putting them 3-4 months behind Azure and GCP"**; the same expert frames it as a deliberate trade — **"AWS is extremely TCO-focused—they didn't prioritize speed but rather TCO optimization."** Quantified: **"Q2 saw only 45-55k Blackwell go live, Q3 and Q4 combined will bring 375k-390k Blackwell online."** Consistent in direction with the SemiAnalysis EFA/cluster-TCO work already on this page.
  - **⚠️ PROJECT RAINIER SIZING — a materially bigger number than either mark on this page, and it is NOT being merged with them.** A data-centre expert: **"Project Rainer totals 4.5GW, more than the 1.3GW media reported, with 3 campuses totaling 4.5GW. The Indiana campus is largest at 2.2GW, mainly completing deployment in 26Q1"**, with the two East Coast campuses expected to complete full deployment in **26Q2-Q3**. This page separately carries **2.25 GW** (JPM "Anthropic Buckle Up," 2025-09-18) and **>1.3GW of IT capacity across three campuses in final construction** (SemiAnalysis, 2025-09-03). ⚠️ **Bases are not stated identically across the three (facility GW vs IT-load GW is unlabelled in the FUNDA source), so all three stay on the page with their sources; do not average them and do not treat 4.5GW as an update to 2.25GW.** See `_meta/assumptions.md` on GW bases.
  - **The pricing-power thesis, which is the note's actual title argument:** AWS's pricing power over the prior five years rested on **Graviton, which FUNDA marks at ~20% better price/performance than other CSPs' CPU products** (⚠️ a different comparator from the 30-40% vs x86 / 40% price-perf marks already on this page — CSP CPU products, not x86 silicon) while also carrying higher AWS margin; under the GPU-cloud narrative AWS was "gradually losing pricing power" and **"needs its own new Graviton for the AI Era"**, with the sting in the tail: **"currently GCP with TPU looks more like the AWS of yesteryear."** That is the bear framing that the 2026 Trainium3/Graviton5 evidence on this page has since argued against — file it as the scorecard's open question, since FUNDA's conclusion was that **only strong TRN3 feedback gives AMZN an unquestioned long-term narrative.**
  - **A retail-side item this page does not otherwise carry — the China merchant tax:** Amazon began **reporting Chinese-merchant transaction data to Chinese tax authorities in October 2025, covering July-September 2025 transactions** (seller identity, sales, refunds, commissions). Chinese sellers exporting via Amazon **without proper export-compliance credentials owe 13% VAT on gross profit**, and greater reporting transparency may raise effective rates for merchants who had under-reported gross profit. FUNDA sizes the potential impact as **on par with tariffs** but explicitly states **no 3Q25 impact and no GMV estimate** — so it is a watch item with no number attached, not a modelled headwind.
  (FUNDA "Preview|AWS 3Q25: AWS Acceleration Ahead, but TRN3 Remains the Key", 2025-10-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Preview_AWS_3Q25__AWS_Acceleration_Ahead_but_TRN3_Remains_the_Key.html)
- **Trainium unit economics — corroborating expert color (AlphaSense expert call, ex-AWS GenAI BD Manager / ex-Cerebras; expert call, interview 2026-03-12 — Q1'26 vintage):** an older-vintage channel read that corroborates (does not supersede) the SemiAnalysis/JPM Trainium economics above — **~500k Trainium chips running Anthropic models; Trainium 25-35% cheaper than Nvidia for the same Anthropic workload** (AWS keeps the spread as a margin hedge); **Bedrock take-rate 20-30% of end-customer spend**; **utilization ~90% currently** (base case 70-80%), contracts outperforming; AWS invested **$8bn total ($4bn + $4bn) into Anthropic**. Pre-dates the Apr-29 Q1'26 print, so vintage-flagged and deliberately kept out of the current post-print intra-quarter log (expert call, interview 2026-03-12 — Q1'26 vintage).
- **Anthropic anchor — asset and dependency (JPM "Anthropic Buckle Up," 2025-09-18):** AMZN is Anthropic's **primary cloud and training partner**, $8B invested to date, distributing Claude to tens of thousands of customers via Bedrock. **Project Rainier = 2.25 GW dedicated supercomputer**, ~5x Anthropic's prior cluster, trained on Trainium — AWS cites 30–40% better price-performance vs comparable GPU instances, which could underpin Anthropic's price competitiveness (relevant given Claude API costs run ~8x Google/OpenAI). JPM frames Anthropic's training infra as "increasingly reliant on Amazon." This is the single largest AI customer concentration in the AWS backlog (and the >$100B deal excluded from the reported $364B). UBS (oAI) adds: with AMZN's Anthropic-specific capacity build, the Anthropic cloud mix "should lean more AWS going forward."
- **OpenAI on AWS — incremental, not core (Barclays "OpenAI," 2025-11-25):** OpenAI signed a **$38B / 7-year AWS commitment (Nov-2025)**, ~$5B/yr, ramping 2026–30. But OpenAI is only ~3% of AWS '28 capacity (AWS 10.6 GW '25YE → 21.2 GW '27YE) — vs 76% for Oracle and 23% for Azure. So the OpenAI relationship is upside optionality on AWS, not a swing factor; Azure remains OpenAI's primary cloud. UBS (oAI) notes Azure's "exclusive" status gives MSFT an edge over AWS at the model layer.
- **Bear:** Capex/ROIC is the swing factor — $200B FY26 capex, capex/rev gone from 7%→20% (Q1'20→Q1'26), and Jassy concedes early-year FCF is "challenged." JPM (AI Capex & Financing, 2025-11-10) pegs AMZN as the **largest hyperscaler capex spender at ~$125B FY25 (up from $78B FY24), reaching ~$150B FY26** — but uniquely **funded entirely from internal cash flow** (no new debt in ~3 years); JPM expects AMZN to re-engage the IG market and/or use sale-leaseback / JV structures around its DC footprint over the next two years. Memory/component cost inflation pressures unit economics. AWS web/SSO metrics softening: Bernstein (Shmulik/Arbsman) flagged non-AI cloud SSO growth "unusually flattened" in May with **4Q non-AI cloud growth at risk** if it persists (briefing 2026-06-06); Bernstein (Shuster) earlier cut AWS web-metric color, 4-week weakening, "Q4 risk" (briefing 2026-06-05). Agentic-commerce threat to retail/ad funnel (JPM Horvers/Anmuth "Retail vs. AMZN" deep dive, 2026-06-16); JPM (AI Capex & Financing) flags Anthropic tension over AI shopping plus reports AMZN aims to automate up to 75% of its labor force — margin upside but unique social/regulatory risk. Ownership signal mixed: **Berkshire fully exited** in Q1; Druckenmiller trimmed to a token position — offset by Ackman ($3.01B, top position) and Tiger (briefings 2026-05-18).
- **Bear overhangs, updated (JPMorgan · Mark Schilsky desk, 2026-06-30):** JPM's sales desk cites a widening list of overhangs on AMZN: a **potentially weakening US consumer**, **2026/2027 capex estimates going materially higher** (driven by higher component pricing), **rising hyperscaler/neocloud competition**, a **possible equity issuance**, and a push to compete in space via **"Leo" (satellite)** (JPMorgan · Mark Schilsky desk, 2026-06-30).
- **BACKFILL / BEAR — AWS's cluster-TCO penalty, quantified (SemiAnalysis "How Much Do GPU Clusters Really Cost?", Nanos/Shan/Cheang/Nishball/Patel, 2026-04-20 — ingested 2026-07-24; **pre-dates the Apr-29 Q1'26 print, so it sits outside the intra-quarter window below** and is logged here as dated structural context):** the most unflattering third-party read on AWS's *cluster-quality* economics on file, and the direct pricing of the EFA weakness diagnosed in the Sept-2025 note above. SemiAnalysis builds cluster TCO from GPU + storage + network + control plane + support + **goodput** + setup + debugging, and compares three archetypes: Gold-tier neocloud (Nebius/Fluidstack/Crusoe), **Hyperscaler (an amalgam of Oracle + Azure + AWS + GCP — but every named modelling assumption in the note is AWS-specific: EFA performance tuning, SageMaker HyperPod slurm/k8s, AWS Unified Operations / Enterprise / Business Support+ tiers, NCCL+EFA debugging)**, and Silver-tier neocloud. Three scenarios, holding $/GPU-hr equal where stated: **(1) 5,184 GB300 NVL72 large-LLM pretrain, 3-yr, $4/GPU-hr matched across all three — Gold 1.00x / Hyperscaler 1.10x / Silver 1.15x**, with the hyperscaler's 10% premium "primarily attributed to the additional cost of support, and setup (EFA performance tuning)," modelled as **a 1-month paid POC plus 1 week/month of two engineers on ongoing network debugging at $200k/engineer**; goodput expense 6.14% / 10.53% / 20.91%. **(2) 2,048 B200 multimodal-RL research — Hyperscaler 1.61x** (GPU price **$3.10 at the ~50th percentile vs $2.40 at the neocloud 25th percentile**, plus a ~**10% orchestration premium** for SageMaker HyperPod slurm/k8s over plain EC2, plus storage and setup). **(3) 512 H200 inference endpoints — Hyperscaler 1.59x**, which "translates almost directly into margin compression" for inference providers: at a $3/M-token blended price, GM falls from **28% at $1.90/hr/GPU to -18% at $3.10/hr/GPU**. The qualitative sting is worse than the numbers: users report that tuning **NCCL + EFA** to reach InfiniBand/RoCE-level performance "can take weeks to months of effort by multiple engineers," and that debugging NCCL+EFA "involves 4 or 5 layers of indirection from their pytorch code, through the driver stack and into the NIC/switch firmware/hardware recipe." **The mitigant SemiAnalysis itself supplies, and the one that matters most for the AWS bull case:** for **fault-tolerant single-node inference the goodput gap collapses to ~0.5% of cluster TCO** — "a real example of why inference providers can find unused capacity from lower tier providers all around the world and use it effectively" — i.e. the EFA penalty bites hardest on large collective-bound *training* and barely at all on the single-node *inference* workloads AWS is increasingly monetising through Bedrock/TaaS. **Vintage caveats:** all pricing inputs are an explicit **August-2025 point-in-time snapshot** (and SemiAnalysis notes GPU rental prices "are going up" as of April 2026 — which cuts in AWS's favour on the relative comparison); **ClusterMAX 2.1 shipped April 2026** and **ClusterMAX 3.0 (B300/GB300, XDR/800G) was in testing over summer 2026**, so the tiering is refreshable. Net read for the book: the AWS reaccel long on this page is a *demand and silicon-cost* story; this note is the standing reminder that AWS's networking/cluster-quality layer is still the weakest link in the stack, and the reason large frontier training keeps leaking to gold-tier neoclouds. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_20_FW_How_Much_Do_GPU_Clusters_Really_Cost_email.html)

Amazon relies on Anthropic for **Alexa shopping, the Kiro coding tool, and the Quick workspace assistant**, and will evaluate cheaper alternatives including **in-house Nova models** (Rothschild & Co, 2026-06-30) — sharpening the softer 06-30 R&Co/Rowan Adley read already logged in the intra-quarter log below (same theme: Anthropic "flexing pricing power over Amazon").

JPM models **370M+ global Prime subs ending 2026 (~371M: ~139M US + ~231M Int'l incl. Business Prime), ~40/60 US/Int'l split, +23M net adds (vs +25M in 2025)**; Int'l still the growth lever — ~19% penetrated across 27 markets (~33% ex-China), and lifting ex-China penetration ~33%→~45% would add **~75M members** (last official disclosure was 200M+ in the 2020 shareholder letter). Value build rests on delivery: US members saved **~$550 avg delivery fees in 2025** (up from $500+ in 2024), **$105B of global delivery-fee savings**, 13B+ items SD/1D globally in 2025 (8B+ US, +30% y/y), ~100M US same-day customers, 85+ SSD FCs carrying the top 90k SKUs. A **$20 US Prime price increase ≈ ~$3B incremental annualized net sales** — JPM now sees the next hike slipping from 2026 **into 2027** (every-~4yr cadence; macro/gas-price pressure), with no expected churn. JPM frames Prime's scale + delivery speed as a structural edge in agentic commerce (agents rank on delivery/price/availability thresholds in LLM queries), reiterating AMZN as a **Best Idea / Analyst Focus List, OW, Dec-26 PT $330 (~32x 2027E GAAP EPS of $10.38)** (JPM/Anmuth — Prime deep-dive, 2026-06-23).
