- **AWS demand "very healthy," the hyperscalers are being paid as model front-ends, and UBS pushes back on the AI-margin bear — read-through from the UBS Microsoft pre-print note (UBS · Karl Keirstead / Taylor McGinnis / Jack Fyda "Microsoft Corp. — Update on Azure, Capex and M365", 2026-07-26):** an MSFT-primary note built on ~a dozen customer/partner conversations, with AWS appearing throughout as the explicit read-across. **(1) Demand:** "**Azure/AWS demand appears to be very healthy**," and "our broader enterprise cloud infrastructure demand checks have been very strong over the last several quarters (**and indeed this has shown up in the sequential dollar adds of AWS, Azure, and Google Cloud**)" — though UBS characterises this specific round as "still healthy albeit with **some pockets of caution**." **(2) The read-across is stated as such:** "Microsoft's comments on the impact of token optimization and open models on 2HCY26 Azure growth will be important, **including as a read to AWS**" — i.e. UBS is telling clients to trade AWS off the MSFT 7/29 call two days ahead of the 7/30 AMZN print. **(3) The revenue mechanic that matters most for AWS and is under-modelled:** "the hyperscalers are benefiting materially from their role as **front-ends to the models and 1P products (such as Claude Code) from OpenAI and Anthropic. Each of AWS, Azure and Google Cloud recognize revenues from those sales, offering a source of revenue upside that goes well beyond serving as providers of compute infrastructure**," and "**Azure as well as AWS and Google Cloud are now materially monetizing OpenAI, Anthropic and Google model API access, as well as 1P product demand.**" That is the Bedrock/TaaS thesis on this page (SemiAnalysis 05-27, Barclays 07-17) stated by a fourth independent house — but UBS attaches the symmetric risk: "**any material customer effort to optimize token consumption and route to open models could impact the growth of these revenue streams in 2H**." **(4) On token optimization, UBS lays out the consensus bull case for AWS explicitly** — "these trends could be a net positive for Azure growth (**and that of AWS as well**)" — on three legs: a three-lab-dominated model market concentrates too much economic power in too few *customers* of the hyperscalers, so a diffuse multi-model market lowers that risk; "**Azure and AWS are already platforms offering access to a host of models, with cloud-hosted inference compute required whatever model is selected**"; and cheaper tokens should drive more tasks. UBS is "sympathetic" but flags one specific AWS-relevant offset: "we have heard from a few customers that one lever to fund higher-than-expected AI token costs is to **optimize elsewhere in their Azure/AWS spend**." **(5) The margin pushback, which is the direct counter to Redburn's Street-low $230 on this page:** MSFT's gross margin fell only 110bps y/y despite $37bn (11% of revs) of AI revenue, and "**We've made the same observation about the margin performance at AWS, Oracle and others — on the surface, AI capex/revs are not landing at the abysmal margin levels that the bears feared.**" **(6) One customer verbatim, AWS-favourable:** Customer 5 — "**Right now we have AWS and Azure. AWS might be 80% of our data. On a y/y basis, our spend on cloud has increased 25%. The key drivers are app migrations in progress, with the big one being SAP to Rise. That's in AWS**" — an 80/20 AWS-over-Azure share split with the marquee enterprise migration workload landing at AWS. Counter-anecdote from the same checks, for balance: Customer 1 has Azure spend contracting 5-10% but is moving to **Google**, not AWS. **(7) Peer-table marks (7/24/26): AMZN $232.11, +1% YTD** — the best YTD performer of the twelve names (vs MSFT -21%, ORCL -41%, mean -33%) — **$2,524,351m market cap, revenue growth +16.6% MRQ / +15.0% CY26E / +13.4% CY27E, non-GAAP P/E 23.9x CY26E / 21.4x CY27E — the highest multiple in the twelve-name set on both years** (vs a mean of 16.1x/14.4x; MSFT 19.2x/16.9x, GOOGL 18.0x/20.0x, ORCL 15.4x/12.6x). i.e. in UBS's own comp set AMZN is simultaneously the best YTD performer and the most expensive name, on mid-teens revenue growth — the premium is being paid for AWS. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ued48088.html)
- **Where the sell-side/buy-side stands:** **GS (Eric Sheridan) Buy, PT $335** (raised from GS's own prior $325; Q2'26 preview, 2026-07-07). **BofA (Justin Post) Buy, PO $310** (briefing 2026-06-18; reiterated 2026-07-07). **Barclays (Ross Sandler) OW, PT $330** (Leo + India, briefing 2026-06-09/06-11; reiterated 2026-07-17 with the margin-agnostic-hyperscaler analysis); Barclays OpenAI note (2025-11-25) reiterates **OW/Positive** ($232.87 ref). **Bernstein Outperform, $315** (Shuster, briefing 2026-06-05). **Citi (Ronald Josey) Buy**. **JPM (Anmuth) OW, Dec-26 PT $330**. **MS (Nowak) Attractive**. **Jefferies (Thill) top 2H26 pick.** **Rothschild & Co Redburn (Alex Haissl / Luke Han) Neutral, PT $230** — the first hard margin bear and the Street-low mark on the page ("Hyperscalers — Margin Call", 2026-07-20). Broad-based bullish (GS's $335 the high of the cluster); the disagreement is on the 4Q non-AI cloud-consumption flag (Bernstein) + Redburn's new margin-disclosure short ($230, the only margin-skeptic desk) vs. the AWS-acceleration narrative everyone else is underwriting.
- **Cost-optimization-over-peak-perf philosophy — Graviton5's PCIe regression as a deliberate TCO choice (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09):** a distinctive AWS positioning point worth flagging explicitly — Graviton5's PCIe lanes actually **regressed from 96 (Graviton4) to 64 (Graviton5, on Gen6)**, because AWS "was generally not deploying configurations using all PCIe lanes"; SemiAnalysis notes "this cost optimization saves Amazon a lot on TCO while not impacting performance" (p.47). This is the same philosophy underpinning the Trainium thesis (cheaper systems via avoiding Nvidia's margin stacking, giving up marketed FP8/FP4 density for lower TCO) — AWS consistently optimizes silicon to its own internal workload mix rather than chasing benchmark-topping peak specs, a design choice bulls read as disciplined and bears could read as under-provisioning risk if workload mix shifts. Same report separately notes Amazon, alongside OpenAI/CoreWeave/"the Cloud Hyperscalers," has the scale/negotiating leverage to secure priority DRAM allocation amid the 2026 shortage (p.68-69). Same cost-first logic shows up on the HBM side: Amazon "emerges as one of the top HBM customers," procuring HBM directly rather than through design partners to lower cost, and **Trainium2e is the socket where Samsung retains the most HBM3E share among hyperscaler ASICs — precisely because Amazon is more willing to trade performance for lower cost** (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall," 2025-08-11, p.4/46). **Read-through — Bernstein (David Dai/Rasgon "The CPU Renaissance? $223bn TAM", 2026-06-17)** independently corroborates the Graviton advantage: Graviton "up to 40% better price-perf vs x86, 20% lower cost, 60% lower energy," **Graviton5 = 192 cores (2x Graviton4)**, and Graviton already **~25% of AWS instances (Dec-24) / 57% of all public-cloud Arm instances** — the Graviton5/192-core spec matches the SemiAnalysis lineage above (not duplicated). [Sources](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/CPU_semianalysis_feb_26.html) · [HBM](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Scaling_the_Memory_Wall__The_Rise_and_Roadmap_of_HBM.html) · [Bernstein CPU](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_on_CPU.html)
- **MS prices the ROIC debate bottom-up — 25-50% incremental GenAI ROIC, and AWS is named as a direct beneficiary (Morgan Stanley "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC", Brian Nowak CFA / Stephen C Byrd / Adam Wood, 2026-07-27; Industry View Attractive):** three new frameworks, all standardised on **1GW of NVDA GB300 — 410,256 chips, $39bn all-in capex/GW ($23bn IT on a 5-yr life + $16bn non-IT on a 15-yr life, at ~$4mn per GB300 rack per MS's Asia semis team's published rack BoM) → ~$6bn/yr of depreciation, 75% utilisation, $7.6bn/GW of total opex (IT dep $5bn + non-IT dep $1bn + energy/other $2bn), 21% tax.** **(1) Hyperscaler GPU rental (IaaS)** at **$8.50/hr → revenue $22.9bn/GW, incremental EBIT $15.3bn (~67% incremental margin), NOPAT $12.1bn, ~31% ROIC**, with the $7.00-$10.00/hr band running **23% → 39% ROIC** and **60% → 72%** incremental margins; MS's conclusion is "~60-70% incremental EBIT margins and ROIC of 25%-40%… we remain bullish **AWS, GCP and Azure**." **(2) Model-enabled API on owned infrastructure** (65% of capacity to inference, 2,750 tokens/sec/GPU, $1.75/mn blended) → **$30bn/GW revenue, $22.7bn EBIT (~75% margin), $17.9bn NOPAT, ~46% ROIC** (band 19% → 63%). **(3) The same API on 3P/rented infrastructure** at **$7.75/hr** → **$40.5bn/GW revenue less ~$28bn of compute cost = $12.6bn EBIT (~31% margin), $10.0bn NOPAT, ~25% NOPAT margin** (band -9% → 32%). **Why framework (3) matters most for this page:** it is the first published attempt to split the token dollar between the model lab and the infrastructure owner, and it lands on **the hyperscaler keeping roughly $28bn of a $40.5bn revenue pool per GW** — the quantified version of the Bedrock/TaaS "front-end" mechanic that SemiAnalysis (05-27), Barclays (07-17) and UBS (07-26) each described qualitatively on this page. It also frames the AWS-vs-neocloud arbitrage: the same GW earns ~46% ROIC owned and ~25% NOPAT margin rented, so the vertical integration AWS has (Trainium/Graviton + Bedrock) is exactly where MS locates the spread. ⚠️ **Carry MS's own caveat — it is the bear's foothold and it maps onto Redburn's $230 short:** "reported near-term incremental margins will remain below that range" because of (a) mix skewed to prior chip generations while GB300 scales, (b) traditional cloud workloads mixed in, (c) opex not in the analysis (headcount, R&D). So the ~67% is a **steady-state GB300-only** figure and is not comparable to Redburn's ~12% Gen-AI-only book margin or the reported ~37-38% AWS EBIT margin. Methodology worth carrying: the **2,000-3,500 tokens/sec/GPU** range is **not a hardware constant** — it is a DeepSeek-V4-Pro 1.6T, FP4, 8K-input/1K-output workload at ~50 tokens/sec/user, with GB300 materially ahead of GB200 at matched interactivity but varying by software configuration; larger models (2.8T Kimi-K3) price higher but throughput lower, so **higher token pricing does not translate proportionately into higher revenue/GW**; and the cost of training is handled by **discounting the compute available for revenue-generating inference while still bearing all the costs**. MS's closing corollary: these returns are "an invitation for competition… further new entrants/open source offerings," which keeps the premium on compute-efficiency innovation and on **the value of compute infrastructure assets**. (Morgan Stanley "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC", 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/INTERNET_20260727_2024.html)
- **The refreshed MS risk-reward page for AMZN — and the AWS growth line is the most aggressive on this page (Morgan Stanley "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC", 2026-07-27):** **Overweight, PT $330.00** = ~25x an average of **$13** of '27/'28 EPS (roll to mid-year), which "implies ~29X '27 EPS and ~22X '28 EPS"; **Bull $400** (~27x an average of $15 of bull '27/'28 EPS), **Bear $210** (~17x an average of $12). **Spot $232.11**, mean consensus PT **$309.55** (range **$207-$370**); consensus rating distribution **94% OW / 6% EW / 0% UW**; options-implied (24 Jul 2026) **P(>$400) ~2.3%, P(>$330) ~11.3%, P(<$210) ~39.7%** — the options market puts the bear case at nearly **4x** the probability of the base, the most skewed of MS's four internet names and a hard number to set against the "consensus long / sentiment 8/10" positioning already logged on this page. Key earnings inputs: revenue **716,924 (2025) → 826,010 (26e) → 957,576 (27e) → 1,109,179 (28e)**; gross profit **361,287 → 428,879 → 524,467 → 638,944**; GAAP EBIT **79,975 → 114,379 → 162,602 → 222,258**; paid-unit growth **10.8 → 11.8 → 9.6 → 9.1%**; and the headline: **AWS revenue growth 19.7% (2025) → 34.9% (26e) → 40.2% (27e) → 36.2% (28e)**. MS vs consensus FY Dec-26e: revenue **826,010 vs cons 823,989** (range 785,000-839,900); EBITDA **223,360 vs cons 212,257**; net income **104,576 vs cons 96,427**; EPS **9.59 vs cons 8.82** (range 7.64-9.93). ⚠️ **Two things on this page it contradicts, and neither should be smoothed.** **(1) MS's 40.2% AWS growth for 2027 is the highest 2027 AWS mark anywhere on this page** — against GS ~35% ('27, 07-07), BofA's 3Q'26 exit of 36% (07-22), JPM's FY26 +32.4%, and the buy-side bogey of 35-36% for the quarter about to print. MS is not marking to the exit rate; it is modelling a *further* acceleration into 2027 before decelerating to 36.2% in 2028. **(2) The out-year EPS is well above the Street desks on this page:** back-solving MS's own multiples gives **~$11.4 of '27 EPS and ~$15.0 of '28** vs JPM's $10.38/$12.95 and GS's $10.15/$12.84 — i.e. the $330 PT (identical to JPM's and Barclays') is reached on a *lower* multiple and *higher* earnings, which is a different bet with the same price tag. Ownership: Inst. owners 55.3% active, HF sector long/short 1.7x, net exposure 11.1%; MS Alpha Models 3/5 BEST (24m) / 2/5 MOST (3m). (Morgan Stanley "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC", 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/INTERNET_20260727_2024.html)
- **🆕 ★ THE AWS EXPERT-CALL FILE — four AlphaSense calls (interviews Feb–Jul 2026), ingested 2026-08-05, and they are the first PRACTITIONER evidence on this page for the unit economics everyone else models top-down.** ⚠️ **Dating discipline first: these are BACKFILL. The interviews run 2026-02-23, 2026-03-12, 2026-04-01 and 2026-07-16 — ALL of them predate the Q2 FY26 print (2026-07-30), so none belongs in `## Intra-quarter` and none of it is news about the quarter. They are filed here because what they contain is STRUCTURAL — utilization, contract shape, Bedrock margin, Trainium strategy — and because three of the four are the same expert (a Former Principal Business Development Manager, World Wide GenAI at AWS, who left in Feb 2024 and subsequently ran GenAI strategy/BD at Cerebras Systems until Jul 2025). ⚠️ **Weight accordingly: a two-and-a-half-year-stale insider view, ex-AWS and ex-[[CEREBRAS]], talking to buy-side clients. Treat as directional colour on mechanism, not as current-quarter fact, and note the [[CEREBRAS]] affiliation when reading the inference-hardware sections.**
  - 🔴 **UTILIZATION IS RUNNING ~10-20 POINTS ABOVE THE UNDERWRITING CASE, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE RETURN STORY (interview 2026-03-12).** The expert's framing: *"To be honest, when we are looking at return, at the end of the day, IT BOILS DOWN TO UTILIZATION. It doesn't matter whether it's AWS, GCP…"* — with base-case models built at **70-80% utilization** and bull/bear cases run at 90% / sub-70%. ⚠️ **The call's own summary states that "recent demand has pushed ACTUAL UTILIZATION CLOSER TO 90%," and that "current cloud provider contracts are OUTPERFORMING BASE CASE EXPECTATIONS due to exceptionally high utilization rates, though… outflows (investments) are also very high."** **This is the practitioner-side corroboration of the MS ROIC framework logged above, which assumes 75% utilization: if the real number is ~90%, MS's ~31% IaaS ROIC is conservative on its single most sensitive input.** The expert also notes the modelled shape — *"My EBITDA, most of the time, for first two years, might be negative"* — i.e. new capacity is designed to be a J-curve, which is the mechanical answer to the Redburn margin short on this page.
  - **CONTRACT STRUCTURE — the risk sits with the customer, not AWS (interview 2026-03-12).** *"Contracts with AI labs and enterprises often include FLEXIBILITY TO UPGRADE TO NEWER GPU HARDWARE, with discounts and terms varying by customer type and contract length; UTILIZATION COMMITMENTS ARE TYPICALLY ON THE CUSTOMER, NOT THE PROVIDER."* ⚠️ **Materially relevant to the "what happens when the cycle turns" bear case: AWS has contracted away the utilization risk on committed capacity, but has also contracted IN a hardware-refresh obligation.**
  - 🔴 **GPU PRICE DECAY, QUANTIFIED — "20-25% over 18 months" (interview 2026-02-23).** *"Price degradation for high-end GPUs typically follows the release of new generations, with a 20-25% DROP OVER 18 MONTHS; NEO-CLOUDS experience SIMILAR PERCENTAGE DECLINES BUT FROM A LOWER BASE PRICE."* Cost structure: fixed (facilities, power, cooling) vs variable, with **hardware maintenance/support the largest single component at ~5% of hardware capex annually**. **Contract lengths: 1-3 years at hyperscalers vs 3-5 years at neo-clouds.** ⚠️ **The 20-25%/18-month decay rate is a checkable number this wiki did not previously carry, and it cuts directly against the Gavin Baker datapoint on [[NVDA]] that B200 rental prices ROSE 50-60% in six-to-seven months. Both can be true — decay is the post-new-generation pattern, the 2026 move is a shortage — but the two should be carried against each other, not averaged.** Structural read on the neocloud comparison: neo-clouds *"operate with LOWER MARGINS and are MORE SENSITIVE TO UTILIZATION AND CUSTOMER CONCENTRATION than hyperscalers"* — the practitioner version of the [[CRWV]] debate.
  - **BEDROCK IS THE MARGIN LAYER, AND IT IS WHERE THE MODEL-AGNOSTIC THESIS GETS PAID (interviews 2026-03-12 and 2026-07-16).** *"EBITDA margin is the key metric for cloud providers, with HIGHER MARGINS ACHIEVED THROUGH MANAGED SERVICES LIKE BEDROCK AND SAGEMAKER, WHICH COMMAND A PREMIUM OVER RAW COMPUTE."* The July call adds the open-vs-closed economics explicitly: *"closed source involves REVENUE SHARING WITH MODEL VENDORS, while OPEN SOURCE ALLOWS THE PROVIDER TO CAPTURE MORE MARGIN but increases operational burden,"* with Bedrock's value being that hyperscalers *"provide value by ABSTRACTING THIS COMPLEXITY."* ⚠️ **This is the sharpest available statement of the UBS/SemiAnalysis/Barclays "front-end" mechanic already on this page — and it says something they do not: the open-source shift is MARGIN-ACCRETIVE to AWS, because it removes the model-vendor revenue share. That inverts the standard token-optimization bear framing.** The July expert's balance: closed-source (Anthropic, OpenAI, Google) *"currently lead in enterprise adoption due to quality, security and operational simplicity"* and will *"retain MAJORITY enterprise share in the near term,"* but open source *"will continue to gain ground, especially for cost-sensitive or customizable use cases."*
  - **TRAINIUM — the strategic intent stated plainly, plus a hypothetical that must NOT be quoted as a forecast (interview 2026-03-12).** *"The provider is HEDGING AGAINST FUTURE MARGIN COMPRESSION by investing in proprietary hardware (Trainium), aiming to offer LOWER-COST ALTERNATIVES TO NVIDIA GPUs and MAINTAIN CONTROL OVER PRICING as supply-demand dynamics shift."* Growing confidence *"especially as ABSTRACTION LAYERS LIKE BEDROCK AND SAGEMAKER REDUCE CUSTOMER SENSITIVITY TO THE UNDERLYING HARDWARE"* — the clearest articulation on this page of *why* Bedrock and Trainium are one strategy rather than two. ⚠️⚠️ **The expert then walked through capacity math — "if we are able to convert 70% of our customer to use [[ANTHROPIC]] model on our Trainium hardware, that means we will need probably TWO MILLION CHIPS… to get to two million chips, we need 27 DATA CENTERS… I need a ONE GW of a power to support it" — and EXPLICITLY LABELLED IT "I'm just giving you a hypothetical number here." IT IS NOT A FORECAST AND MUST NOT BE CITED AS ONE. It is logged only because the STRUCTURE of the calculation (customer-conversion % → chips → datacenters → GW) is how AWS actually sizes capacity, which is itself the useful disclosure.**
  - ⚠️ **THE DISSENTING CALL, and it is the one worth arguing with (Senior Staff Software Engineer at Pinterest — an AWS CUSTOMER, not an insider; interview 2026-04-01).** *"Skeptical about AWS's ability to catch up with competitors in AI due to CULTURAL AND LEADERSHIP CHALLENGES, and perceives AWS as LAGGING IN AI STRATEGY AND INNOVATION,"* with *"GCP seen as having BETTER AI TOOLING, PRICING, AND A MORE DATA-CENTRIC CULTURE; Azure's main advantage is ENTERPRISE BUNDLING."* On Bedrock specifically, the customer view contradicts the insider view above: *"managed AI services like AWS Bedrock are useful for RAPID PROTOTYPING and early-stage companies but are EXPENSIVE AND NOT COST-EFFECTIVE FOR LONG-TERM, LARGE-SCALE USE; access to GPU CAPACITY and OPERATIONAL EXPERTISE are the main value propositions."* And on the sector: *"the ROI of AI investments [is] STILL UNPROVEN for most businesses, with current infrastructure costs OUTWEIGHING PRODUCTIVITY GAINS,"* expecting *"several years before clear value is realized."* ⚠️ **Do not net this against the ex-AWS expert's Bedrock-margin case — they are answering different questions (AWS's margin per Bedrock dollar vs the customer's cost per Bedrock dollar) and BOTH can hold: Bedrock being expensive for the customer is WHY it carries the premium margin. The genuine disagreement is the AI-strategy/culture claim, which is one customer's opinion and sits against AWS's +36.7% Q2 growth and $496bn backlog.**
  - **Sources:** [Utilization / capacity planning (2026-03-26)](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/AMZN_-_ExpertCall_document_dated_05-08-2026.html) · [Open-source models & Bedrock economics (2026-07-30)](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/AMZN_-_ExpertCall_document_dated_05-08-2026_2.html) · [GPU unit economics & neocloud comparison (2026-03-09)](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/--_-_ExpertCall_document_dated_05-08-2026_3.html) · [Pinterest customer view (2026-04-06)](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/--_-_ExpertCall_document_dated_05-08-2026.html)
- **🆕 ★ THE CAPEX QUESTION REFRAMED AS AN OPTION — the first framework on this page that prices AMZN's spend as two assets at once (FUNDA "Deep|LLM: RSI Is the Most Important Variable, and Compute Is the Deepest Moat", 2026-08-05):** FUNDA's argument is that the market is running the wrong calculation — it evaluates AI through an *application* lens (find the next coding-like use case, then check whether current revenue covers capex) while the labs are underwriting **recursive self-improvement (RSI)**, so capex should be read as a **dual investment: real current returns from AI-driven cloud growth PLUS an option on having sufficient compute when the RSI threshold arrives.** ⚠️ **The AMZN evidence it leans on is already on this page from the primary transcript and is NOT re-stated here as new** (AWS **+37%, its fastest in eighteen quarters and its fifth consecutive quarter of acceleration**, and Jassy's line that even after raising 2026 capex to roughly **$220B** it **"still isn't enough to meet all of this year's demand"**) — the framework is the value-add, not the figures. **The transmission FUNDA draws is the Bedrock thesis stated from the demand side:** rising token consumption → paid conversion at **AWS Bedrock** (named alongside Microsoft Copilot and Vertex AI) → higher cloud revenue and profit → further capex; "the narrative that AI revenue cannot support the compute buildout is being displaced by a different loop: tokens, paid conversion, cloud growth, reinvestment." **The group arithmetic that does NOT close, stated by the bull:** the four largest cloud providers guided to roughly **$725B of 2026 capex, up ~77% y/y, which absorbs the majority of their operating cash flow**, and the multi-track compute commitments (Stargate's **$500B / 9GW** plan, Anthropic's Google deal for **up to 1M TPUs**) "still do not close" on a current-ARR payback — FUNDA concedes that is exactly the skeptics' calculation and answers it with option value rather than with revenue. **⚠️ The part to carry into the book is the risk tiering, because FUNDA separates strategic necessity from shareholder return explicitly:** "an arms race that is individually rational can compress industry-level returns, because when everyone must expand, the cost of expansion gets bid up and consumes part of the expected gain" — with **GPU economic life of a few years** and **token prices falling roughly 10x a year** as the leakage channels, a substantial share of the value potentially settling "as consumer surplus rather than producer profit." **Three scenarios, keyed to RSI timing: (A) threshold ~2027 — FUNDA's MOST LIKELY case; compute assets run at full utilisation, the strategic value is realised, and value concentrates in the one or two labs that cross first AND in their compute supply chain; risk LOW. (B) threshold slips to 2029 or later — capability keeps improving but a capex DIGESTION period intervenes; utilisation determines who is hurt first, depreciation and generational obsolescence steadily erode returns, and highly levered participants with weak cash flow are forced out; risk MEDIUM, "warrants serious weight." (C) longer still, or continual learning stays unsolved for an extended period — the pre-investment beyond genuine inference demand faces IMPAIRMENT; risk HIGH, treated as a tail scenario.** **How it maps onto this page:** scenario A is the tape AMZN is currently priced on and is consistent both with Jassy's own payback arithmetic (<3yrs to server break-even, ≥5-6yr life, demand > supply through 2027) and with MS's 25-50% GenAI ROIC work (07-27); **scenario B is the same digestion risk Redburn's Street-low $230 prices, arriving from a capability-timing angle rather than a margin-disclosure angle** — and note that the participants FUNDA says get forced out first are "highly levered… with weak cash flow," which on this page's own funding evidence (JPM 06-16: ~$85bn of new capacity arranged in <6 months without touching equity, ~$143bn cash) is the neocloud cohort rather than AMZN — see [[CRWV]]. Tape context FUNDA is writing into: **July 2026 was the sharpest drawdown of this cycle — semiconductors' worst month since 2002, SOXX down roughly 20%, and more than $1trn of AI-linked market value off** — which FUNDA reads as "a repricing of narrative disagreement rather than an inflection in the capability curve." ⚠️ **Third-party framework: no AMZN rating, no price target, no estimates. Carry it as a lens on the capex debate, not as a mark.** (FUNDA "Deep|LLM: RSI Is the Most Important Variable, and Compute Is the Deepest Moat", 2026-08-05) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Funda_on_RSI.html)
- **Token-optimization read-through — neutral:** UBS is **neutral** on hyperscalers re: token optimization — enterprise down-tiering to cheaper SLMs/open-source Chinese models (Qwen, Z.ai, Kimi, DeepSeek) could weigh on frontier-model API revenue growth and AI price sensitivity could limit near-term GPU pricing, but the hyperscalers are already multi-model platforms facing a wall of AI demand. For AMZN, **AWS Bedrock is the multi-model platform** (already offering a drop-down of open-source Chinese models alongside Anthropic/OpenAI; Databricks/Anthropic run on AWS), and any enterprise down-tiering still needs AWS-hosted inference compute — being model-agnostic framed as an edge "similar to the advantage secured by the likes of Snowflake and MongoDB that were multi-cloud while AWS and Microsoft were pushing their native databases" (UBS "The Ramp in Token Optimization" / Keirstead-Arcuri, 2026-06-23).

## Catalysts / what to watch
- **Prime Day — actual print:** sales **+9% y/y to $26.4bn** (Numerator data), stable discounts y/y but **avg order size -11%**, a possible mix shift into essentials/grocery (BofA · Justin Post/Kushalappa Berera team, 2026-06-30). Flags a risk of **$7-8bn of sales pulling forward from Q3 into Q2** given the Prime Day timing shift (BofA · Justin Post, 2026-06-30). (Superseded the pre-event BofA estimate of ~$22B GMV / ≈+5% y/y from the 2026-06-18 briefing — see Changelog.)
- ~~**Q2 FY26 print**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-07-30 (AMC): bull won.** Rev $200.6B / OI $27.5B / AWS +36.7% / AWS margin 39.4% / backlog $496B, all above bogeys; stock +8-10% AH. The offsets: Q3 revenue guide below Street (Prime-Day optical), FCF −$9B vs cons −$1B, FY26 capex ~$200B → ~$220B on memory inflation. BofA's call that revenue would print at/above the high end of the guide was right (guide $194-199B, actual $200.6B).
- **MS AMZN 2Q26 group callback with Brian Nowak — 2026-07-31, 8:30 AM ET** (MS Wigg, 2026-07-30).
- **Does the ~40% AWS exit rate arrive, and is it supply-capped?** Buy side now underwrites ~40% for Q3 after +36.7% in Q2 (UBS Dwyer, 2026-07-30); management said demand exceeds supply for both 2026 and 2027, so the binding constraint is capacity delivery, not bookings. MS was already the Street high at 40.2% for 2027 pre-print (Nowak/Byrd/Wood, 2026-07-27).
- **FY27 capex number — still not given by management, but the Street has now marked it, and the marks disagree by ~$40bn.** Management gave FY26 (~$220B) and "heavy capex over next few years" but no 2027 figure. Post-print: **JPM models $365B FY27E (+65%) / $445B FY28E** (Anmuth, 2026-07-31) and **MS raised its forward path to $325B/$350B** (Nowak, 2026-07-31) — both well above JPM's own prior ~$300B/~$350B path (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16). The spread between the two houses *is* the memory-cost pass-through assumption, which is precisely the variable management declined to quantify. Watch whether the ~$20B memory increment that hit FY26 compounds into the eventual FY27 guide. JPM's number would put AMZN capex just below GOOGL's for 2027.
- **Whether the FCF trough is Q2'26.** By management's own framework (spend ~2 years ahead of monetization), FCF stays negative until the current data-center cohort opens. −$9B in Q2 vs −$1B consensus is the first quarter where the market has a marked-to-reality number to extrapolate.
- **AWS backlog trajectory** — does the >$100B Anthropic deal show up and does non-frontier-lab breadth keep building.
- **Amazon LEO commercial launch (Q3'26)**; capex capitalization begins 4Q26 (Barclays). Signed commitments: Delta (≥half fleet from 2028), JetBlue, AT&T, Vodafone, NASA, Apple (direct-to-device via Globalstar).
- **Trainium3 ramp** — volume skewed to the air-cooled NL32x2 SKU in '26; key technical gating items: N3P leakage/defect-density (could push timelines), Hynix/Micron HBM3E qualification, and **LNC=8 software support (mid-2026)** for mainstream researcher adoption (SemiAnalysis, 2025-12-04). **Trainium4 in 2027** (UALink + NVLink-Fusion tracks, Alchip backend) / **possibility of selling Trainium racks externally** (Jassy: "good chance in the next couple of years").
- **Trainium3 head-node ramp on Graviton5 (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09, p.48):** AWS uses Graviton CPUs internally for chip-design EDA/CI-CD flows (Graviton designing Graviton/Trainium/Nitro), and **Trainium3 accelerators will use Graviton CPUs as head nodes at a 1 CPU : 4 XPU ratio** — initial Trainium3 versions run on Graviton4, with future Trainium3 clusters moving to **Graviton5**. Directly ties Graviton unit-economics to the Trainium3 AI-infra ramp — watch Graviton5 volume/yield as a gating item for Trainium3 cluster scale-out. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/CPU_semianalysis_feb_26.html)
- **DRAM-shortage negotiating leverage (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09, p.68-69):** Amazon named (with OpenAI, CoreWeave, and "the Cloud Hyperscalers") among the few buyers with sufficient scale to secure priority DRAM allocation amid the 2026 shortage — a potential relative-share tailwind for AWS's CPU/server fleet build-out vs smaller cloud/OEM buyers starved of supply. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/CPU_semianalysis_feb_26.html)
- **Non-AI cloud consumption** into 4Q — Bernstein's flagged risk.
- Agentic-commerce execution (Rufus: MAU +115%, engagement +400% y/y).
- **Russell reconstitution flow (Jefferies, 2026-06-08):** the June-26 Russell reclass moves **~600bps of Amazon into the Russell 1000 Value benchmark** (AMZN goes ~zero in 1000 Growth), part of a broad Mag7 growth→value style shift — a mechanical flow event (forced selling of "winners," tax/turnover implications) and a window for active managers to fund the SpaceX/mega-cap IPOs by trimming Mag7 (Jefferies Russell rebal call, 2026-06-08). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_08_jeff_rtya_rebal_8_jun_26.html)

## Risks
- **Capital intensity / ROIC**: $200B FY26 capex (~$150B AWS-driven per JPM 2025-11-10), depreciation drag on AWS margin, suppressed near-term FCF; potential shift toward IG debt issuance / sale-leaseback financing (Q1'26 call; JPM AI Capex & Financing, 2025-11-10; 10-K capex-cycle disclosures). **Sector-wide AI-debt overhang: Google/Amazon/Microsoft/Meta/Oracle have together piled up ~$700bn of debt on AI data-center spend (@Hedgeye, 2026-07-13)** — a hyperscaler-cohort framing of the capital-intensity turn.
- **AI demand concentration**: backlog skewed to a few frontier labs; Anthropic (Project Rainier 2.25 GW, $8B AMZN invested) is both the franchise asset and a single-point dependency, plus reported AI-shopping tension between the two (JPM, 2025-09-18 / 2025-11-10).
- **Component/memory cost inflation** and supply constraints squeezing infra unit economics (Q1'26 call); Trainium3 HBM3E sourcing shift to Hynix/Micron and N3P yield are gating items (SemiAnalysis, 2025-12-04).
- **Competition**: Azure (MSFT AI ARR >$37B, +123% — Bernstein 2026-04-30) and GCP (backlog surge, TPU rack sales) contesting AI cloud; Trainium still trails Nvidia on FP4 and CUDA-ecosystem maturity (SemiAnalysis, 2025-12-04); agentic commerce threatening retail/ad funnel. **Trainium adoption-breadth read-through (Google Principal Architect Arthur Khessin, expert call 2026-06-23):** a competitor's framing — Trainium is characterized as having **"only one single customer" (Anthropic)** with scaling friction because customers must rewrite algorithms per custom ASIC, illustrating the difficulty of broad custom-silicon adoption absent massive internal consumption (the structural contrast vs Google's own-workload TPU pull). Source is a Google exec talking his own book, but a useful bear-side counterweight to the >$225B Trainium-commitment bull.
- **🆕 Third-party colocation exposure to the Texas/ERCOT audit — contracted MW safe, incremental MW not (Bernstein · Gautam Chhugani, "AI Infra/miners: Texas data center audit to drive more MW scarcity", 2026-08-04, read-through; subject is the miner/AI-infra cohort, not AMZN):** Cipher Mining (CIFR) has **700 MW UNDER CONSTRUCTION for AWS and Fluidstack**, inside an approved **1.2 GW that Bernstein says is explicitly unaffected by the audit** — but **2 GW of CIFR's 3.2 GW TOTAL PIPELINE depends on ERCOT "Batch Zero" approval for energization, and ERCOT has PAUSED that review** (results had been due 2026-08-07) after Governor Abbott directed the PUCT and ERCOT to audit all data-center projects in the interconnection queue. **So incremental AWS capacity from this counterparty beyond the contracted 700 MW is exposed to the audit delay; the 700 MW itself is not.** ⚠️ **MW bases labelled: 700 MW = under construction · 1.2 GW = grid-approved · 3.2 GW = total pipeline — none of these is energised IT load (`_meta/assumptions.md`).** Cipher Mining / CIFR is not a wiki-covered name. See [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md).
- **🆕 The capex-as-option risk, tiered by RSI timing (FUNDA "Deep|LLM: RSI Is the Most Important Variable, and Compute Is the Deepest Moat", 2026-08-05):** a framework risk rather than a company-specific one, but it names the variable AMZN's own payback arithmetic assumes away. If the RSI threshold slips to **2029 or later** (FUNDA's scenario B, "warrants serious weight"), capability keeps improving but a **capex digestion period** intervenes — utilisation determines who is hurt first, and **depreciation plus generational obsolescence steadily erode returns** (GPU economic life "a few years"; token prices historically falling ~10x/yr, so value leaks to consumer surplus). In the tail (scenario C, continual learning unsolved for an extended period) the **pre-investment beyond genuine inference demand faces impairment**. Even in the base case (threshold ~2027) FUNDA is explicit that shareholder returns are not automatic: **"an arms race that is individually rational can compress industry-level returns."** ⚠️ **Read against management's own disclosure this is a utilisation / asset-life / pricing risk, not a demand risk — the "<3 years to break even" figure presumes the ≥5-6 year useful life and the ≥5-year contracted terms both hold, and it is silent on generational obsolescence and on token-price decay.** Framework only; FUNDA carries no AMZN rating, PT or estimates.
- **Regulatory/antitrust, FX, energy/power availability, geopolitical** (10-K FY25 risk factors; LEO/supply-chain execution risk); labor-automation (up to ~75%) carries social/regulatory exposure (JPM, 2025-11-10).

The monetisation stack is named: serverless API tokens (Bedrock), accelerator rental, RAG-driven storage/database revenue, and security — with the framing that "API TOKEN REVENUE ON OPEN MODELS MAY BE A LOSS LEADER (LIKE MILK IN A GROCERY STORE)" while generating "MORE CORE CLOUD INFRASTRUCTURE ATTACH PER WORKLOAD." MS keeps AMZN OW explicitly on lowest-cost-to-serve. GS (08-12) from the margin side: "the benefit of increasing workloads overall… is MORE THAN OFFSETTING that negative mix shift. It's a VOLUME GAME for them… they are playing as being THE AGNOSTIC PLAYER."** ⚠️ **The debit lands on the LAB layer, not here — MS sees open weights compressing model-layer token pricing, which is exactly where Amazon's own frontier model will have to live** | **✓ confirms** — and it upgrades Bedrock's model-agnosticism from a hedge to the business model itself. ⚠️ But the claim to audit is ATTACH, not tokens: if open-weight API revenue is a deliberate loss leader, the proof has to show up in storage / database / Graviton pull-through, none of which AWS discloses |

**Síntese do quarter:** _(new window - Q2'26 opened 2026-07-30)_ The print resolved the single biggest bear objection of the July de-rating — that hyperscaler AI ROI was unprovable — with a specific quantified payback framework (<3yrs to server break-even, 5-6 server generations per 30-yr shell) rather than qualitative reassurance, and it did so on an accelerating top line (AWS +36.7%, fastest in 18 quarters, margin 39.4%). The offsetting negative is cash: **FCF −$9B vs consensus −$1B**, and FY26 capex went ~$200B → ~$220B with the increment attributed to **memory cost inflation** — i.e. the same memory squeeze that hit AAPL's gross margin the same evening is now showing up in hyperscaler capex dollars, not just in COGS. Open question for the window: whether the ~40% AWS exit-rate the bulls now underwrite survives contact with supply. **Updated 2026-07-30 (run-inbox, primary transcript): one material downgrade to the quality of the print, and one upgrade to the option value. Downgrade — ~$1.2bn of the $27.5bn OI is discrete (tariff refunds + an energy-derivative fair-value mark), and management's own clean AWS margin expansion is +520bps y/y, not the +650bps reported; roughly a third of the OI beat vs Street is non-operating, and the AWS margin line is where it sits. That does not break the bull case (AWS still expanded ~520bps clean while capex ripped) but it does mean the "39.4% margin" number quoted across every desk note is flattered. Upgrade — Trainium as merchant silicon moved from sell-side hypothetical to management-stated live conversation, and Amazon confirmed it is building its own frontier model to sit on the Bedrock shelf next to Anthropic's and OpenAI's. Net: the ROI framework still looks like the more durable fact of the print, but the margin proof is softer than day-one framing, and the pricing mechanism Amazon described for recovering memory inflation (new agreements only) is weaker than the tranche-reset mechanism Microsoft's IR described the same day — see the Cost pass-through row above.** **08-03 (run-inbox — Fubon · Shang/Arthur, hosted by Jefferies) adds the first Asia-supply-chain read since the print, and it lands on the two things the print left thinnest. On the silicon: an independent PRODUCTION-side check — total Trainium chip output ~1.6-1.7mn (2026) → ~3mn (2027) — arrives within ~10% of the page's existing UBS Trainium3 DEMAND mark (1.8mn/2.8mn), which is about as good a corroboration as this program gets given AWS discloses no units; both marks stay, on their own bases. On memory cost, which is the weakest link in the print's story (capex raised to ~$220B with the increment attributed explicitly to memory, and a pass-through mechanism limited to new agreements): the ODMs who actually procure the parts see NO 2027 memory spec change for AWS by name, are budgeting at full rack prices, and are raising their own capex to fund buy-and-sell of GPUs — i.e. no de-spec escape hatch, but also no sign of demand hesitation at the higher prices. The open item is architectural rather than financial: Trainium 4 (~mid-to-Q3 2027) may disaggregate the networking tray from the compute tray, ODMs say AWS has not decided, and Hon Hai joins as a new AWS rack supplier — worth tracking because it would change the switch/optics content of an AWS rack, and because the same ODM is already stretched on Google's TPU rack assembly. **08-05 (FUNDA · Deep\|LLM) adds no numbers to the quarter and is not meant to — it supplies the frame the ROI debate has been missing, and it is the frame we should be arguing inside. FUNDA's claim is that the market's application-lens calculation (find the next coding-like use case, check whether revenue covers capex) is the wrong one, and that the right one reads capex as a DUAL investment: real current returns from AI-driven cloud growth PLUS an option on having enough compute when the recursive-self-improvement threshold arrives. AMZN is the cleanest current-return leg of that argument (AWS +37%, fifth straight quarter of acceleration, and Jassy saying ~$220B "still isn't enough"), and AWS Bedrock is named as one of the three paid-conversion layers in the token → cloud-revenue → capex loop. The discipline it imposes is the risk tiering, and it is the part to hold: strategic necessity is NOT shareholder return — scenario A (RSI threshold ~2027, FUNDA's base case) validates the current tape; scenario B (2029+) is a capex DIGESTION period in which utilisation, depreciation and generational obsolescence do the damage and levered weak-cash-flow players are forced out; scenario C (continual learning unsolved) puts spend beyond genuine inference demand at risk of impairment. Note what that does to the page's existing spread of views: it is the first framework here that makes the AMZN bull case (Jassy's <3yr payback, MS's 25-50% ROIC) and the Redburn $230 bear case functions of the SAME variable — RSI/adoption TIMING — rather than of disagreements about margin disclosure. On this reading the thing to monitor is not the next quarter's AWS growth rate but whether the capability line keeps compressing (FUNDA's own indicators: monthly model iteration, one month pre-training-to-release, three weeks to a price cut), because that is what decides which scenario AMZN's ~$220B is being spent into.** **RE-EVALUATED 2026-08-12 (/run-inbox, hyperscaler cluster — 7 back-dated sources, 07-22 → 08-12). The quarter's open question ("does the ~40% AWS exit-rate the bulls underwrite survive contact with supply?") now has a two-part answer, and the second half is the one that matters. ① ON LEVEL — the margin is better-quality than the print made it look, because it is a blend of a 40-45% core business, a ~15% Anthropic-training loss-leader, and a Bedrock line whose NET revenue accounting makes it structurally accretive (GS 08-12; corroborated mechanism-by-mechanism by MS 08-04). The recent expansion is a MIX effect — the low-margin training workloads lapping — not a cost miracle, which makes it more repeatable, not less. ② ON DIRECTION — every new source caps it. GS models margins DOWN on depreciation as capex mixes into short-life servers; Nowak says explicitly "I do not think AWS margins are going to get to 50," they will "march into the 40s on a reported basis" and then reinvest. ➜ THE PRACTICAL EDGE: a model carrying ≥50% reported AWS margin in 2028 is now offside two houses that both like the stock, and the reconciliation item is depreciation schedule, not demand. ③ THE PRICING WINDFALL IS REAL AND IT ACCRUES HERE, NOT TO THE CONTRACT PLAYERS — Jefferies (08-10) sizes gigawatt monetisation moving from $8-12bn at the start of the year to $30-50bn now ("3 to 5x in the span of six months"), and the structural point is that hyperscalers hold on-demand/spot/short-contract exposure while [[ORCL]] and [[CRWV]] locked five-year unit economics. ④ TWO HARD FACTS ADDED: AWS contract duration per the 10-K went 5 years → 6 years (vs [[MSFT]] commercial RPO at 2-3 years, mixed with M365); and Trainium partner feedback flipped — "Trainium 1 or 2… not great feedback, but with the NEW ONES coming on we've heard GREAT feedbacks." ⑤ THE ONE GENUINELY NEW BEAR, from the customer side: an Anthropic infrastructure practitioner (07-28) expects a frontier lab to be running ~30% of its capacity on OWN data centres within three years, 60% hyperscalers, 10% neoclouds — the labs are actively engineering their way out of being downstream, and the fleet is fully fungible across GPU/TPU/Trainium/AMD.** **RE-EVALUATED 2026-08-13 (2 sources: Morgan Stanley · Nowak 08-12, UncoverAlpha 08-13). The quarter's newest debate — whether cheap open-weight models deflate the token line the whole AWS AI thesis rests on — got its first direct answer, and it lands in Amazon's favour: MS's argument is that compute is the scarce resource, that lower per-token pricing arrives with substantially more volume, and that "EBIT DOLLAR GROWTH MATTERS MORE THAN PERCENTAGE MARGINS HERE," with API token revenue on open models explicitly framed as a possible loss leader — "like milk in a grocery store" — that pulls storage, database, security and rental attach behind it. MS keeps AMZN OW on lowest-cost-to-serve and names custom silicon plus request batching (deepest enterprise footprint ⇒ highest volume of requests to batch) as the two throughput levers only a hyperscaler owns. That is the same conclusion GS reached from the margin side on 08-12, reached independently. ⚠️ The offsetting item is the sharpest one on the page against the silicon story, and it comes from outside the sell side: UncoverAlpha (08-13) argues Trainium's cost-per-token competitiveness "is due to Amazon PUSHING FOR VERY LOW MARGINS," not only to the chip — which is consistent with GS's ~15% training-book EBITDA and which quietly discounts the merchant-Trainium optionality the Q2 call opened, since a merchant price has to carry a vendor margin the captive price does not. ➜ NET FOR THE WINDOW: the open-weight bear is answered on the cloud layer and left standing on the model layer, and the thing to audit next is ATTACH (storage/database/Graviton pull-through), which AWS does not disclose. On the marks: MS's base PT is unchanged at $335 but BOTH scenario legs moved up ($400→$410 bull, $210→$215 bear) and the '27 AWS growth estimate went 40.2% → 40.8%, the page-high.** **🆕 Updated 2026-08-16 (wiki-ingest): two additions, neither of which moves a reported number. (1) MS · Nowak now publishes the SIZED bridge from Jassy's '$1trn AWS' remark to a share price — ~8 incremental GW/yr, monetization rising from ~$8 to $12-15 of REVENUE per incremental watt, 30% LT EBIT margin → ~$500bn company EBIT in 2034-36 → ~$500/share by YE27 (base PT still $335). That is a LONG-DATED frame, not a quarter call: it changes nothing about the '26/'27 print and everything about how the bull case gets underwritten. (2) The supply side of Trainium is now confirmed from the back-end design house (JPM on Alchip), with Trn4 taping out end-2026 — which means the next real test on custom silicon is whether AWS INSOURCES the wafer allocation, not whether the chip lands.**

OVERWEIGHT, PT $335 (base, unchanged), BULL PATH $500 by YE27** | valuation | **bull** | **★ THE FIRST TIME THIS PAGE CARRIES A SIZED BRIDGE FROM JASSY'S "$1 TRILLION AWS" REMARK TO A SHARE PRICE, and the swing variable is monetization per watt, not GW.** MS quotes the Q2'26 call verbatim: *"We long believed AWS could become a few hundred billion dollar revenue business and now believe it'll be at least double that, and very possibly be a $1 TRILLION ANNUAL REVENUE BUSINESS for us in time"* — vs **~$170bn annualized today (~6x)**. **The bridge: (i) capacity — MS estimates AMZN brings on 6 GW in '26 and 8 GW in '27, and assumes ~8 incremental GW/year thereafter as the base case, conceding "limited visibility beyond '27"; (ii) monetization — AWS is monetizing at ~$8 per incremental watt (FY26E), and at $12/incremental watt $1trn arrives in 2035, at $14-15 as early as 2034; (iii) margin — if AI tracks core cloud at ~30% EBIT margin, $1trn of AWS revenue implies ~$300bn AWS EBIT and ~$500bn TOTAL AMZN EBIT in 2034-36, a 16-20% company-wide EBIT CAGR; (iv) valuation — capitalise that $500bn at ~21x EV/EBIT (a ~10% discount to the ~23x peer average) and discount back at a 10% WACC to '28 → ~$500/share by YE27, i.e. 80-100% upside.** The mirror-image framing MS offers for the bear: **AMZN today trades at ~11x that discounted '35 EBIT (~$265bn), "a ~50% discount to its peers."** ⚠️ **UNIT DISCIPLINE: "$/incremental watt" here is REVENUE per watt (monetization), NOT capex per watt — do not net it against $/GW build-cost anchors (see [[themes/ai-datacenter-power]]).** ⚠️ **Note what is load-bearing: the whole thing rests on management's own "margins and returns in AI tracking what we saw with core… actually a little ahead" claim. If AI-era AWS margins settle below core, both the EBIT and the multiple leg break together.** |

| 08-16 | **The Information · Catherine Perloff — "Nebius and CoreWeave Tout Short-Term Cloud Deals, While AWS Goes Long"** | competicao | **bull** | **A clean statement of the contract-duration split that the market is currently rewarding on BOTH sides, which is the interesting part.** The neoclouds ([[NBIS]], [[CRWV]]) pitched investors this week on **shorter-term contracts** to "cash in on surging prices for AI compute capacity amid a crunch for GPUs" — and both stocks moved sharply higher. **AWS used its own call to argue the opposite: it is selling "MOST of its AI capacity via FIVE-YEAR DEALS," with Jassy framing the long duration as what "ensure[s] AWS will generate enough revenue to make money on its data center build-out" — and "Amazon's stock is up OVER 11% from its July 30 call."** ⚠️ **Single-outlet trade-press item, no numbers disclosed. But it sharpens the page's open question: long contracts de-risk the depreciation schedule and give up the spot upside; short contracts do the reverse. Both narratives can't be right about where pricing goes — watch which one holds when GPU supply loosens.** |
| 07-13 (backfill) | **Morgan Stanley · Meta Marshall / Joseph Moore — "Bringing More Light to Scale-Up Networks: An Updated Scale-Up Primer" (GAP-FILL from the primary note)** | produto | **bull** | **Backfilled 2026-08-10 from the 39-page primary. This page already carried RNG at tweet level ("random-graph vs fat-tree", @SemiAnalysis_ 07-14); MS quantifies it and dates the rollout.** ★ **RNG (Resilient Network Graphs) — the numbers: "reducing router count by 69%, improving throughput by up to 33%, and projecting a 40% REDUCTION IN NETWORK EQUIPMENT ELECTRICITY CONSUMPTION."** The mechanism: a **flatter quasi-random graph** replacing the hierarchical fat-tree, using **ShuffleBox passive optical components** and **"Spraypoint" routing** to make flat networks practical at production scale — conventional fat-trees *"require additional router layers, can concentrate congestion near the top of the hierarchy, and create more fragility when important routers fail."* ★ **The deployment timeline is the part that reframes it from a paper to an asset: first quasi-random production network live near DUBLIN at END-2024, refined through two further deployments, and "becoming the DEFAULT ARCHITECTURE FOR MOST NEW AWS DATA CENTERS GLOBALLY BY APRIL 2026."** MS: *"Moving from initial deployment to default architecture in about 18 months suggests a HIGH LEVEL OF CONFIDENCE in the design and highlights how aggressively AWS is reworking the physical network layer to support AI-scale infrastructure."* ⚠️ **The honest debit, and it is MS's own:** RNG *"requires significant adaptive routing / software expertise, a move away from recent history"* and, given its proprietary nature, *"we don't yet see major adoption beyond Amazon"* — **a genuine AWS differentiator, not a merchant-networking TAM.** Read-through: a **40% cut in network power** is a real TCO lever on the ~$220B capex programme this page tracks, and it lands on the **passive-optical** side (ShuffleBoxes), not on switch silicon. **Also from the note, on the Trainium roadmap: "we view Trainium 5 (post-2028) as the earliest likely opportunity for AWS"** to adopt optical scale-up — AWS is placed **one to two years behind NVIDIA**, later than AMD (MI550/MI650, 2027-28) and the AVGO ASIC customers (2028+). Near-term, **Trainium 3 uses PCIe via [[ALAB]] Scorpio-X** (shipping, ramping 2H26) with MS expecting **AWS to pursue "a dual-track strategy for Trainium 4, supporting both UALink and NVLink Fusion."** MS estimates AWS Trainium at **~11% of 2026 accelerator units** (vs NVDA GPU 48%, Google TPU 24%). (MS · Marshall / Moore, 2026-07-13; see [optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md)) |
| 08-13 | **UncoverAlpha · Rihard Jarc — "Nvidia and the Hyperscalers: High Stakes Poker Game" (independent Substack, PAID — NOT a bank; no rating, PT or estimates)** | competicao | **mixed** | ★★ **THE ONE CLAIM HERE THAT ATTACKS THE TRAINIUM COST ADVANTAGE AT ITS SOURCE, and it should be read against every "~30% better TCO / 30-40% better price-perf" mark on this page: "Trainium, given how young the project is, is HIGHLY COMPETITIVE WITH NVIDIA ON A COST-PER-TOKEN BASIS; MUCH OF THIS IS DUE TO AMAZON PUSHING FOR VERY LOW MARGINS." The mechanism named is cultural, not technical — Amazon "often likes to DRIVE MARGINS TO ZERO DURING ADOPTION PERIODS, as they have an obsession with focusing on customers before reaching scale and passing [most] of the economic benefits to their customers. THERE IS A REASON WHY [[ANTHROPIC]] LIKES TRAINIUM FOR INFERENCE TASKS."** ➜ **If the cost-per-token gap is part price and part silicon, two things follow: (a) it is CONSISTENT with GS's 08-12 decomposition — a ~15% EBITDA Anthropic-training book IS a priced-to-zero book — and (b) it DISCOUNTS the merchant-Trainium optionality Jassy opened on the Q2 call, because a merchant price has to carry a vendor margin that a captive transfer price does not. Logged as an argument, not a number; no unit economics are published.** ★ **OUTSIDE CORROBORATION of marks already on this page: chips (Trainium + Graviton) "over **$25B in annualized revenue**, growing triple digits"; Graviton "used by **98% of the top 1,000 EC2 customers**"; and Amazon framing "its semiconductor design business as **ONE OF THE NEXT PILLARS OF THE AMAZON EMPIRE**" across multiple recent calls.** ⚠️ **ONE MARK THAT DOES NOT MATCH AND IS FLAGGED RATHER THAN MERGED: "Project Rainier — the [[ANTHROPIC]] cluster — is SCALING FROM 500,000+ TRAINIUM CHIPS TOWARD 1M." This page carries JPM's ">1M Trainium2 chips (Project Rainier)" as a level and SemiAnalysis's "just under 1M Trainium2 in the largest [campus]." UncoverAlpha has the 1M as a DESTINATION, JPM as a STATE — different tense on the same programme. Both stay, neither adopted.** ★ **THE STRATEGIC FRAME, and it is the useful part for the capex debate: on the Q2 calls all three hyperscalers described capex as an OPTION — long-lead assets (land, shells, power, cooling, "assets that last 15-25 years") committed years ahead, chips as "SHORT-LIVED ASSETS" bought only months before a datacenter comes online, with Jassy talking about "DIFFERENT CAPITAL CYCLES" for land/power vs chips. Same late-binding disclosure this page already carries verbatim (Jefferies 08-02) — here it is read as a deliberate joint move to commoditise [[NVDA]], with the endgame stated explicitly: "copy the playbook on the CPU book with Graviton, where AMAZON'S OWN GRAVITON CHIP NOW HANDLES MOST OF THE CPU WORKLOADS IN AWS."** ★ **Net-new and directly relevant to the Cost pass-through / Capex rows: "ALL OF THE HYPERSCALERS ARE ALSO GOING DIRECT TO MEMORY MANUFACTURERS to secure memory capacity" — i.e. the memory inflation behind the ~$220B guide is being answered by procurement structure, not by de-spec (consistent with the Fubon 08-03 ODM check that no 2027 AWS memory spec change is planned).** ⚠️ **BASE FLAG on the one group number: AMZN + [[MSFT]] + [[GOOG]] together "will spend around **$600B in CapEx this year**" — THREE companies only, versus BofA's ~$860bn CY26 "cloud capex" (08-02, wider cohort) and MS's ~$800bn Big-4 + [[SPCX]] terrestrial (08-04). Different cohorts; do not net.** ⚠️ **Independent Substack, paywalled beyond the halfway point; the sections on [[NVDA]]'s neocloud backstops, the $500B Apollo/BlackRock/Blackstone/Brookfield/GS/KKR financing partnership and the AMD/OpenAI options belong on [[NVDA]] and are not folded here.** (UncoverAlpha, 2026-08-13) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Nvidia_and_the_Hyperscalers_High_Stakes_Poker_Game.html) |
| 08-12 | **Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak (with Julian Herrera / Gregory Gao / Nikhil Javeri / Kavya Narayanan) — "How Could Open-Weight Models Impact GenAI ROIC?" · Internet · North America, Industry View Attractive** | margem | **bull** | ★★ **THE PUNCHLINE IS A NAMED PREFERENCE AND AMZN IS IN IT: "investors should remain focused on the hyperscaler (or owner of compute) that has the LOWEST COST TO SERVE (through CUSTOM SILICON, unique 1P models/offerings and/or other software/hardware advances). WE REMAIN OW AMZN AND GOOGL FOR THESE REASONS." Note who is absent from that sentence — [[META]] sits on the MODEL-LAB side of this note, which is where MS puts the pricing pressure.** ★ **THE QUESTION IT ANSWERS — do cheap open-weight models break hyperscaler ROIC? MS says no, for FOUR reasons: ① "COMPUTE IS THE SCARCE RESOURCE, and enterprises rely on hyperscalers to provide compute necessary for open-weight inference workloads"; ② hyperscalers monetise to maximise ROIC and lower per-token pricing "may come with SUBSTANTIALLY MORE VOLUME at lower per token or GW unit economics — **EBIT DOLLAR GROWTH MATTERS MORE THAN PERCENTAGE MARGINS HERE**"; ③ both layers stay focused on token throughput and incremental unit economics; ④ all GenAI workloads "drive ATTACH of other services and incremental profitability."** ★ **THE FOUR MONETISATION PATHS — this is the Bedrock thesis argued from the other end: ① serverless API token revenue (Bedrock/Foundry/Vertex), ② accelerator rental for customers self-deploying, ③ RAG — "connecting open models to proprietary data… driving STORAGE AND DATABASE REVENUE," ④ security (governance, guardrails, identity, monitoring). And the framing to carry: "while API TOKEN REVENUE ON OPEN MODELS MAY BE A LOSS LEADER (LIKE MILK IN A GROCERY STORE), open models have the potential to generate MORE CORE CLOUD INFRASTRUCTURE ATTACH PER WORKLOAD."** ➜ **Same conclusion GS reached from the margin side the same day ("it's a VOLUME GAME for them… they are playing as being THE AGNOSTIC PLAYER"), arrived at independently from unit economics. Two houses now agree the open-weight mix shift is not an AWS margin problem.** ★ **WHY AMZN SPECIFICALLY, and one lever here is net-new: "we believe hyperscalers drive their own throughput gains to the model layer from both PROPRIETARY HARDWARE (custom silicon via TPUs/TRAINIUM) and SOFTWARE (hyperscalers have the DEEPEST ENTERPRISE FOOTPRINTS and thus the HIGHEST VOLUME OF REQUESTS TO BATCH)." Request batching as a scale advantage has not been on this page before, and it is the one throughput lever a neocloud cannot buy.** ★ **THE IaaS ROIC TABLE, reprinted unchanged — the point being that the band SURVIVES the open-weight price deck (1GW GB300, 410,256 chips, 75% utilisation, $7.6bn/GW opex = $5bn IT dep + $1bn non-IT dep + $2bn energy/other, 21% tax): at $7.00 / $8.50 / $10.00 per GPU-hour → revenue/GW $18.9bn / $22.9bn / $27.0bn, incremental EBIT margins 60% / 67% / 72%, NOPAT $8.9bn / $12.1bn / $15.3bn, **ROIC 23% / 31% / 39%**. 🔴 **REFRESHED AMZN RISK-REWARD — the BULL AND BEAR MOVED, the base did not: OW, **PT $335** (~25X P/E on an average of **$14** of '27/'28 EPS, implying ~28X '27 and ~22X '28); **BULL $410** (~27X avg $15 of bull '27/'28 EPS) — **RAISED from $400**; **BEAR $215** (~17X avg $12) — **RAISED from $210**. Spot **$272.27** (08/11/26); mean consensus PT **$319.83** (range **$207-$400**, from $309.55 / $207-$370); **94% OW / 6% EW / 0% UW**; options-implied **P(>$410) ~8.3%, P(>$335) ~23.1%, P(<$215) ~26.1%** — the market still prices the bear as marginally MORE likely than the base, which is the same asymmetry MS's July exhibit showed.** ★ **DRIVERS, and the AWS line is a straight upgrade: revenue **716,924 → 833,352 → 973,667 → 1,134,182** ('25-'28e, from 826,010 / 957,576 / 1,109,179); gross profit 361,287 → 433,328 → 534,679 → 656,405; GAAP EBIT **79,975 → 116,753 → 166,089 → 227,300** ('28 from 222,258); paid unit growth 10.8% → 12.9% → 9.6% → 9.1%; **AWS revenue growth 19.7% → 37.4% → 40.8% → 36.8%, up from 34.9% / 40.2% / 36.2% — the 40.8% for '27 is the new page-high AWS mark.** ⚠️ **BASIS FLAG, DELIBERATELY NOT RECONCILED: MS vs consensus FY Dec-2026e — sales **833,352 vs 828,245** (range 815,866-841,977); EBITDA **224,292 vs 216,000** (197,862-235,567); net income **146,557 vs 137,430** (95,986-148,986); EPS **$13.43 vs $12.69** ($8.79-$13.51). Both the MS and the consensus EPS sit ~40% above the same exhibit three weeks earlier ($9.59 vs $8.82, 07-27) and far above this page's BBG snapshot of **$9.60 CY26E**. The likeliest reconciler is GAAP below-the-line investment gains — the [[ANTHROPIC]]/[[OPENAI]] stake revaluations this page already tracks at ~$17bn in 1Q alone — landing in a GAAP series the BBG line does not carry. | 08-12 | 🔴🔴 **Goldman Sachs · Alex (US Internet team) — Capstone-hosted call on CLOUD MARGINS. THE FIRST SUB-SEGMENT MARGIN DECOMPOSITION OF AWS THIS PAGE HAS EVER CARRIED, and it reframes the whole "AWS margin quality" debate** | margem | **bull** | ★★ **THE DECOMPOSITION (GS estimates — Amazon discloses none of it): ① TRADITIONAL / NON-AI WORKLOADS — "much higher margin than they're currently reported… UPWARDS OF 40, 45% PLUS EBITDA MARGINS." ② [[ANTHROPIC]] TRAINING WORKLOADS (Project Rainier) — "VERY, VERY LOW MARGIN, we think upwards of like VERY ROUGHLY 15%-ISH EBITDA MARGINS." ③ BEDROCK — "quite high incremental margins… probably CLOSER TO THE 40, 45% PLUS rather than 15% plus," and the reason is the accounting: "they report revenues NET, not gross… the incremental costs are just the INFERENCE-RELATED COST, basically the depreciation. It's not like they report revenues gross and then have to pay out a rev share to Anthropic — they ALREADY NETTED IT OUT ON THE REPORTED REVENUE LINE."** ➜ **This is the cleanest available answer to "is the AWS margin real?": the reported ~39% is a BLEND of a 40-45% core business, a ~15% training business that is a strategic loss-leader, and a Bedrock line whose accounting makes it structurally accretive. The recent margin expansion is explained as the low-margin training mix LAPPING, not as a one-off.** ⚠️ **GS is nonetheless modelling AWS margins DOWN from here, and is explicit that the driver is singular — asked "why are you seeing worse margins going forward, is it just D&A or something else," the answer was "REALLY REALLY D&A. That's really the bigger driver."** Mechanism (quoting Jassy's own 2Q framing back): capex has been going into shells, land, buildings and power (no or very long depreciation) and is now mixing toward **servers, networking and chips at much shorter useful lives** — so the depreciation flow-through "starts to compound this year." GS deliberately **does not underwrite the 50-55%+ incremental margins the buy-side is extrapolating**, and models **non-depreciation AWS expenses growing "low double digits to mid-teens."** ★ **STRUCTURE, useful for modelling: EC2 is ~50% of the CORE (non-AI) AWS revenue line — and core is ~80% of total AWS (GS confirmed the "latter" reading of its own EC2 note when asked directly).** ★ **ON PRICE: the EC2 price-increase thesis is framed as "POTENTIAL UPSIDE DRIVERS… we're not necessarily baking in the benefit." Amazon's philosophy is low-cost provider / volume game; where they WILL take price is to pass through "significant cost input inflation — memory pricing, things like that" (Sundar said the same), NOT to expand margin. On the GPU/training line specifically GS "would be SURPRISED if they dramatically take margin or raise price on those TRAINING-related workloads" because it would cannibalise the long-dated inference opportunity.** ★ **BEDROCK'S REAL MOAT, in GS's words, is the ROUTING LAYER, not model access: "there's an orchestration and routing layer… that routes the query to the best model from a price-performance balance… if I'm Goldman Sachs, I don't want to put the onus on my employees to maintain all of these different APIs and know which model is best for which query."** And the open-weight mix shift is **NOT** a margin problem — "the benefit of increasing workloads overall… is MORE THAN OFFSETTING that negative mix shift. It's a VOLUME GAME for them… they are playing as being THE AGNOSTIC PLAYER." ★★ **THE CALL: "we are VERY BULLISH on the hyperscalers at these levels and SPECIFICALLY AMAZON… Amazon is still an UNDERAPPRECIATED AND DISLOCATED STOCK versus the value longer term and is THE CLEANEST PURE-PLAY WAY in our view versus [[GOOG]] or certainly versus [[META]] and some of the other hyperscalers to play this theme of increasing value going to the INFRASTRUCTURE LAYER of AI."** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_12_gs_call_on_clouds.html) |

An internal-efficiency directive at the exact moment capex is at a record.** | supply / margem | **mixed** | **★ THE ITEM: AWS is instructing engineers to reduce CPU waste in the face of a capacity crunch (The Information, 2026-08-07).** ⚠️ **Headline and framing captured; the internal detail, the scope of the directive and any quantification are NOT in the captured text. Logged as a pointer, not a datapoint.** **WHY IT MATTERS ANYWAY, and why it is filed here rather than dropped: this page carries CY2026 AWS capex around $214bn versus Jassy's ~$220bn guide, on a thesis that spend is demand-constrained. AN INTERNAL DIRECTIVE TO RECLAIM EXISTING CPU CAPACITY IS THE BEHAVIOUR OF A SUPPLY-CONSTRAINED OPERATOR, NOT A DEMAND-CONSTRAINED ONE — it says the marginal cheapest capacity available right now is capacity AWS already owns.** ⚠️ **Two readings and they matter differently: (a) BULLISH — demand exceeds even a record capex plan, so utilisation and pricing hold; (b) BEARISH ON THE CAPEX LINE — efficiency gains reclaimed internally are capacity NOT purchased, which is the mechanism [[NVDA]] bears invoke. This page should not pick one on a headline alone; both are logged and the discriminator is whether AWS capex guidance moves at the next print.** ✅ **Consistent with the same-week evidence from a competitor's disclosure: [[MSFT]] "has been UNABLE TO LEVERAGE THEIR ACCESS TO OPENAI MODELS FOR THEIR API BUSINESS FOUNDRY, OR FOR THEIR APPLICATIONS LIKE COPILOT" for lack of compute (SemiAnalysis, 08-07) — two of the three largest clouds simultaneously rationing internal compute is a supply signal, not a demand one.** (The Information, 2026-08-07) |

Cipher Mining / CIFR is not a wiki-covered name. Cross-ref [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md). That's mean they put the computing and networking together… they have a computing tray and also networking tray"** — i.e. T3 stays on the integrated compute+networking rack topology, converging with [[NVDA]]/[[AMD]] rather than diverging ([themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md)). 🔴 **Trainium 4 is where it changes, and it is explicitly UNCONFIRMED: "for Training 4 we heard that will be networking will be INDEPENDENT, but still NOT READY OR CONFIRMED FROM ODM, because major builder AWS they will be weighing. Anonymous FinTwit, no model published; logged as a framework, not a number.** (@SouthernValue95, 2026-08-02, 265 likes) |
| 08-05 | **FUNDA · "Deep\|LLM: RSI Is the Most Important Variable, and Compute Is the Deepest Moat"** | capital | **mixed** | ⚠️ **Third-party FRAMEWORK, not a mark — no AMZN rating, PT or estimates, and the AMZN figures it cites are the page's own primary-transcript numbers, deliberately not re-added to the body.** **The claim: the market is running an application-lens calculation (where is the next coding-like use case, does current revenue cover capex) while the labs are underwriting RECURSIVE SELF-IMPROVEMENT — so capex should be read as a DUAL INVESTMENT, real current returns from AI-driven cloud growth PLUS an option on having sufficient compute when the RSI threshold arrives.** AMZN is the current-return leg: **AWS +37%, "its fastest in eighteen quarters and its fifth consecutive quarter of acceleration," and Jassy that even after raising 2026 capex to roughly $220B it "still isn't enough to meet all of this year's demand."** **AWS Bedrock is named (with Microsoft Copilot and Vertex AI) as the paid-conversion layer in the loop FUNDA draws — "tokens, paid conversion, cloud growth, reinvestment"** — which is the Bedrock/TaaS thesis on this page arriving from the demand side. ⚠️ **The honest half, and the reason this is logged mixed rather than bull: the group arithmetic does NOT close on current ARR — the four largest cloud providers guided to roughly $725B of 2026 capex, +77% y/y, "absorbing the majority of their operating cash flow," and multi-track commitments (Stargate $500B/9GW; Anthropic–Google up to 1M TPUs) do not pay back on today's revenue. FUNDA answers with option value, then separates it from shareholder return explicitly: "an arms race that is individually rational can compress industry-level returns," with GPU economic life of a few years and token prices falling ~10x/yr as the leakage.** 🔴 **The three-scenario frame, keyed to RSI timing — carry this: (A) threshold ~2027, FUNDA's MOST LIKELY — full utilisation, strategic value realised, value concentrates in the first movers and their compute supply chain; LOW risk. (B) threshold slips to 2029+ — a capex DIGESTION period; utilisation decides who is hurt first, depreciation and generational obsolescence erode returns, levered players with weak cash flow are forced out; MEDIUM risk, "warrants serious weight." (C) longer, or continual learning unsolved — spend beyond genuine inference demand faces IMPAIRMENT; HIGH risk, tail.** **Why it matters for the book: it is the first framework on this page that makes the AMZN bull case (Jassy's <3yr payback; MS's 25-50% GenAI ROIC, 07-27) and the Redburn Street-low $230 bear case functions of the SAME variable — adoption/RSI timing — rather than of a disagreement about margin disclosure. On FUNDA's own scenario-B language the participants forced out first are "highly levered… with weak cash flow," which on this page's funding evidence is the neocloud cohort ([[CRWV]]), not AMZN.** Tape context: **July 2026 was the sharpest drawdown of the cycle — semiconductors' worst month since 2002, SOXX down ~20%, >$1trn of AI-linked market value off** — which FUNDA reads as "a repricing of narrative disagreement rather than an inflection in the capability curve." Cross-ref [themes/hyperscaler-capex](themes/hyperscaler-capex.md), [[MSFT]], [[GOOG]]. (FUNDA "Deep\|LLM: RSI Is the Most Important Variable, and Compute Is the Deepest Moat", 2026-08-05) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Funda_on_RSI.html) |

(2026-08-12, 08:00 PM GMT)](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/INTERNET_20260812_2000.html) — Internet · North America, Industry View **Attractive**. The follow-up to "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC" (07-27) and "Global Thematics: Weighing In: Open-Weights Models & 3 States of the World" (08-03). **Four reasons hyperscaler unit economics survive cheap open-weight inference (compute is the scarce resource; volume over percentage margin — "EBIT dollar growth matters more"; token-throughput focus; attach of other services), plus the four monetisation paths — serverless API tokens via Bedrock/Foundry/Vertex, accelerator rental, RAG-driven storage/database, security — with the loss-leader framing ("like milk in a grocery store") and "more core cloud infrastructure attach per workload."** Throughput levers attributed to hyperscalers specifically: **proprietary custom silicon (Trainium/TPU) and request batching off the deepest enterprise footprints.** IaaS ROIC table on 1GW GB300 (410,256 chips, 75% utilisation, $7.6bn/GW opex, 21% tax): $7.00/$8.50/$10.00 per GPU-hr → **$18.9/$22.9/$27.0bn revenue/GW, 60/67/72% incremental EBIT margins, $8.9/$12.1/$15.3bn NOPAT, 23/31/39% ROIC.** Lab-layer deck: base **~$1.75 per million tokens**, throughput **2,000-3,500 tokens/sec/GPU** (DeepSeek-V4-Pro 1.6T on GB300), still **~20-60% ROIC on 1GW of owned GB300**. **"We remain OW AMZN and GOOGL for these reasons."** **AMZN risk-reward: OW PT $335 (~25X avg $14 of '27/'28 EPS; ## Changelog

None of these figures is energised IT load and none is comparable to the GW/capex figures elsewhere on this page.** Bernstein's broader conclusion is directionally consistent with the "capacity wars" bullets already on the page: the audit "throttles speculative data center pipeline and makes genuine sites with development history more valuable" → approved MW re-rate, time-to-power lengthens for new builds. ⚠️ **Miner tickers written as PLAIN TEXT — no wiki pages exist for CIFR/WULF/CORZ/CLSK/RIOT/IREN/MARA, so no links were created.** Cross-filed [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md).
