The DEMAND-VALIDATION rule alongside it is the more durable claim: "90%-95% OF THE THING WHAT WE BUILD IS BASED ON THE NEEDS OF A CUSTOMER. If we do not have those needs VALIDATED BY CUSTOMERS OR BY THE NAMED ENTITIES, WE WOULD NOT EVEN BUILD IT. There are only 10%-15% of the things AWS builds which is NOT PARTICULARLY CALLED OUT BY A CUSTOMER… Without having that 80% CONFIDENCE, they would not go into that direction."** ⚠️ **The illustrative "out of 10,000 [Trainiums], probably 8,000 ARE GOING TO BE USED BY [[ANTHROPIC]]" is explicitly hypothetical — "I am just using the name here" — and must NOT be carried as an Anthropic allocation figure.** ✅ **PRICING DISCIPLINE UNDER SCARCITY — a direct answer to the "does AWS surcharge in a shortage" question, and it is a NO: "There is NO POINT IN AMAZON LIFE I HAVE SEEN WHERE WE WOULD CHARGE MORE CUSTOMERS BECAUSE OF THE LACK OF CAPACITY. We would DEFER that… 'we will give you 5,000 this week, and we will STAGGER IT'." Cost is agreed before capacity is allocated, via discount programmes.** ⚠️ **This CONTRADICTS the AWS "+20% fee hike on select GPU workloads effective July 1" that BofA reported on 2026-06-30 and that sits on this page. Both are retained: a list-price action on a SKU and a refusal to raise agreed prices on a committed customer under allocation are not the same thing, but the expert's "never" is stated more absolutely than the evidence supports.** 🔴 **THE INFERENCE-HARDWARE CLAIM THAT MATTERS TO [[NVDA]] AND IS THE MOST CONTESTABLE THING IN THE CALL: "WHEN WE ARE LOOKING AT INFERENCE AS A PROFILE, YOU DO NOT NEED THE HIGH-END GPU FOR INFERENCE… you can LIVE BY A100, WHICH WAS CREATED SIX YEARS AGO, AND IT WILL HAVE MORE OR LESS THE SAME LATENCY ON INFERENCE WHAT YOU HAD FOR H200 OR B200." Shortage framing to match: "IT IS NOT LIKE 2022, where everyone was fighting for GPU. Today, you can get a GPU… H100, EVEN B200 IN SOME CASES. A100 ARE AMPLE."** ⚠️⚠️ **NOT ADOPTED. It is flatly inconsistent with the memory-bandwidth-bound decode economics this wiki carries from [[NVDA]] and [tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md) — where per-user interactivity scales with HBM bandwidth per token and generation-over-generation bandwidth gains are 2-3x. It is also a Q2-vintage view of a market that has tightened since. Logged as an attributed practitioner claim and flagged as the one item in this call a modelled view should reject.** ✅ **STRUCTURAL MOAT COLOUR, useful and consistent with the page: the security/sovereignty argument for going through a hyperscaler rather than direct to a lab ("typically 12-18 MONTHS to vet a cloud provider"; "Amazon will GUARANTEE THAT YOUR DATA WILL NEVER LEAVE AWS PERIMETER… a HARD GUARANTEE IN THE LEGAL CONTRACT"); the mechanic that makes it possible — "[[ANTHROPIC]] WILL HAND OFF THAT MODEL, THE BINARY, TO AWS… 'here is my Claude Opus. You run it for us on the GPU or Trainium'… [[ANTHROPIC]] IS ACTUALLY NOT IN THE LOOP, PER SE. It's PURELY AWS CONTROLLED AND MANAGED"; the enterprise-tooling gap at the labs ("[[OPENAI]] would not have this. Even Anthropic would not have this, because THEY ARE NOT YET INTO THE ENTERPRISE CUSTOMER MINDSET"); and data gravity as the durable lock ("if your data is in AWS, quite likely you will NOT MOVE THAT DATA… JUST BECAUSE THEY HAVE A BETTER MODEL, because NOW THE MODEL PARITY IS EVEN").** **Named references: Perplexity has trained on SageMaker "for almost three years now — ALL THE MODELS OF PERPLEXITY IS COMING FROM SAGEMAKER FACTORY"; BloombergGPT and the Falcon family trained by the expert's team; Bedrock scale given only as a range the expert would not commit to ("probably OVER 100,000 CUSTOMERS, MAYBE ONE MILLION… not the exact number I know of")** ⚠️ **— the page's disclosed Q1'26 figure of 125k+ Bedrock customers is the mark that stands; the expert's range is not used.** (AlphaSense expert call — Former Principal Business Development Manager, World Wide Generative AI, AWS; interview 2026-06-15, published 2026-06-29; received via the P: team drop 2026-08-07) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/AMZN_-_ExpertCall_document_dated_07-08-2026_7.html) |
| 08-03 | **Morgan Stanley · Erik Woodring — "Global Cloud Capex Tracker: Spending Acceleration Continues"** | capital | **bull** | 🔴 **AMZN MADE THE BIGGEST CAPEX MOVE OF THE FOUR, AND THE STATED CAUSE IS MEMORY: "AMZN made the MOST DRAMATIC MOVE, bumping its CY26 CASH CAPEX TO ~$220B FROM ~$200B — WITH MEMORY COST INFLATION AS THE CULPRIT — and STILL FLAGGED IT WON'T BE ENOUGH TO SATISFY DEMAND."** ⚠️ **Read the mechanism, because it is the crux of the whole 2026 capex debate: a $20bn raise attributed to input-cost inflation rather than to incremental capacity is NOT the same as a demand-driven raise. MS's Asia team frames the sector-wide version the same way — hyperscalers "raising spending to ABSORB [component] costs RATHER THAN CUTTING UNIT VOLUMES." Bullish for [[MU]]/[[SKHYNIX]]/[[SAMSUNG]]/[[SNDK]] pricing, neutral-to-negative for the units of everything else in the same rack. Cross-file [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [themes/hyperscaler-capex](themes/hyperscaler-capex.md).** **Operating detail: "AWS delivered its FASTEST GROWTH RATE (+37% Y/Y) IN 18 QUARTERS, supported by both AI and traditional cloud workloads, with mgmt emphasizing that AI adoption is INCREASINGLY DRIVING INCREMENTAL CONSUMPTION ACROSS STORAGE, DATABASES, NETWORKING, AND CORE CLOUD SERVICES." Capacity: AMZN states "2026 CAPACITY WILL REMAIN INSUFFICIENT TO MEET DEMAND and that MUCH OF ITS 2027 CAPACITY IS ALREADY RESERVED."** **On ROIC — the most specific disclosure of the four: "AMZN emphasized that MOST AI CAPACITY IS CONTRACTED ON MULTI-YEAR TERMS, with SERVER INVESTMENTS ACHIEVING BREAKEVEN IN <3 YEARS and meaningful FCF generation thereafter, while mgmt now sees a path toward a SUBSTANTIALLY LARGER long-term AWS revenue opportunity."** ⚠️ **Counterweight already on this page, and it belongs alongside: Jassy's own late-binding language — "we typically purchase these A FEW MONTHS BEFORE putting them into service… IF THE DEMAND ISN'T THERE, WE WON'T SPEND THE CAPITAL." Multi-year contracted capacity and a few-months purchase window are both true and describe different parts of the stack; do not net them.** **Sector marks: 2027 consensus cloud capex ~$1.2T (+30% y/y), up ~$170B/15pts post-C2Q; MSe $1.4T, 17% above consensus; capital intensity approaching 35%, an all-time high.** (Morgan Stanley · Erik Woodring / Brian Nowak / Joseph Moore / Meta Marshall, 2026-08-03) |
| 08-03 | **Deutsche Bank · Brad Zelnick — "Public Cloud & AI Update: ROIC becoming more visible"** | capital | **bull** | **AWS named on the right side of the margin evidence: "both GOOGLE CLOUD AND AWS POSTED Y/Y OPERATING MARGIN EXPANSION (adj. for chip sales and a 1x derivative gain)," while [[MSFT]]'s Intelligence Cloud margins were only stable. Group: aggregate revenue ~4.5% above consensus, growth "SURGING A RECORD ~10PTS SEQUENTIALLY TO +50% Y/Y @cc" for AWS/Azure/Google Cloud/OCI, with a record $14BN OF Q/Q NET REVENUE GROWTH (+~150% y/y) "despite ongoing supply constraints that are NOW SEEN AS LIKELY TO PERSIST THROUGH CY27." AI is a mid-20s% of group mix, from low-20s last quarter and ~10% a year ago. CPUs) and models to optimize performance."** ⚠️ **The CPU point is the one worth carrying for the Graviton/Trainium thesis on this page — DB explicitly flags "an INCREASING RATIO OF CPU TO GPU USAGE" as supportive of long-term ROIC. DB's own caveat: "rising memory prices have arguably YET TO FULLY START FLOWING THROUGH."** (Deutsche Bank · Brad Zelnick / Bhavin Shah, 2026-08-03) |
| 07-29 | SemiAnalysis · "LEGO Datacenters" | capital | bull | **AWS is the reference case for operator-led modularization and, per SemiAnalysis, "shows the fastest scaling buildout currently, adding almost 3.9 GW of capacity to the end of 2025."** **Project Houdini** re-cuts the standard data-hall map into a factory-built **~45-ft skid on low double-drop trailers engineered to stay under bridges** (a factory-built white-space product): "**cuts deployment from as much as 15 weeks to roughly 2–3 weeks and can eliminate more than 50,000 on-site electrician hours per module**," skids built in **Houston, Salt Lake City and Topeka**, early deployments **Texas and South Bend**, target **~25 weeks from construction start to first server room**. Governance differentiator: the factory process "**requires separate validation of the design and the physical build**," with **Cupertino Electric ([PWR](PWR.md))** the main partner on the build validation and AWS's design partner on the spec. On the shell, "**AWS is now rolling out at very large scale their own modular design codenamed 'SAMDC'**" — narrower, repeatable structures organised around the systems inside, giving less building per MW and fewer field interfaces. Model context: full modular on a 50 MW liquid-cooled hall = **~36% (7-9 months) faster, ~8% cheaper (~$13.5 vs ~$14.6mn/MW)**; an earlier month is worth **~$500k/MW** to an owner-operator (~$200mn / ~$4mn/MW on an ~8-month lead). Caveat carried: "go-live is the latest of building ready, power available, and GPUs delivered." |

Trainium (Garman: "now powers >50% of Bedrock token usage") is the natural inference-optimized offtake; Graviton4 heads Trn3 nodes; AWS now has large CPU/Graviton deals with Anthropic, OpenAI and Meta. Corroborating detail from the same week: UBS's CLS preview notes **server/TPU supply-chain headwinds from last quarter have been remediated** and **800G switch demand at META and AMZN is tracking above prior expectations**. Fenske: AWS "firing on all cylinders" — Anthropic workloads, Trainium traction, and **AMZN raised fees by 20% on select GPU workloads as of 7/1** (a small subset, but a supply/demand tell — already on the page from Post's 06-30 note, reiterated here). BAC card data has US online spending accelerating in Q2 (Street models NA revenue accelerating 12% → 14%). The valuation stat Fenske leads with: **AMZN GAAP EPS has compounded ~60% '23-'26 vs the ~30% CAGR consensus forecast at the start of 2023 — 2x the expected earnings growth — while the P/E de-rated from 35x to 21x.** Positioning: LOs trimming AMZN (with MSFT/META/NVDA/GOOGL) since ~Dec-2025. |

Two datapoints beyond the AWS silicon thread: **DRAM is "the tightest supply chain constraint," with shortages expected to last until at least 2H2028** while **CPU supply is stable and not a bottleneck**; and **open-source plus Chinese models capture up to one-third of token usage**, concentrated at the low end, with frontier models keeping high-end, creative and enterprise work. Also notes **startups may accept slower inference to cut cost** — relevant to AWS's price-performance positioning against frontier-model APIs. Single expert, no financial disclosures — directional colour, not a datapoint. Cross-ref [NVDA.md](NVDA.md), [MU.md](MU.md). **Quarter synthesis:** the debate migrated from "is AWS reaccelerating?" (already answered: +28%, silicon validated, margin inflecting) to the sustainability of the funding and the health of the non-AI core — JPM shows FCF collapsing while AMZN pre-funds ~$85bn of the FY27 ramp, and the only crack in the bull is the non-AI cloud SSO weakness that Bernstein sees threatening 4Q. **06-29: BofA Justin Post confirms AWS compute pricing power** — +20% CB price hike (July 1), effective prices +22% from 2022 trough, adds 1–2ppt to 2H AWS growth. Prime Day +9% ($26.4B) confirms consumer demand resilience. **06-30: BofA flags a Q3-guide risk from the Prime Day July→June shift (~$7-8bn out of Q3), and R&Co reports Anthropic renegotiating how Amazon pays to use its models next year — the first crack of pricing-power tension in the marquee AWS-Anthropic relationship.** Separately, deep-dive silicon research (SemiAnalysis CPU + HBM reports, folded in 06-30) broadens the custom-silicon validation beyond Trainium alone: Graviton5 (192 cores/172B transistors) is now wired directly into the Trainium3 ramp as head nodes, and both the CPU (PCIe lanes cut 96→64) and accelerator (Trainium2e's HBM3E sourcing) sides show the same deliberate cost-over-peak-spec philosophy — a coherent, distinctive positioning point for the AWS silicon thesis, alongside Barron's corroborating the $168B AWS 2026 revenue and $215B 2-yr capex scale. **06-30/07-01 update: the AWS-pricing-power and silicon-demand confirms keep piling up (BofA's +20% GPU-workload fee hike restated with more color on Bedrock/OpenAI/Anthropic drivers; UBS lifts Trainium3 unit demand to 1.8mn/2.8mn '26E/'27E; MS models AMZN's 2027 IT-capacity build at ~5GW, ahead of META but behind GOOGL; Barclays ties AMZN into the new "Meta Compute" external-cloud story) — but two new bear-relevant threads emerged in parallel: (1) valuation/sentiment is lagging the operational print (MS: AMZN -5% in a month, now 25x '26 EPS / -22% vs TTM avg; JPM's sales desk lists a widening bear overhang sheet — weak-consumer risk, higher '26/'27 capex ests on component pricing, neocloud competition, possible equity issuance, and the "Leo" satellite push); (2) the Anthropic relationship, the single largest AI-customer concentration in the AWS backlog, now has a concrete repricing mechanism attached (token-based billing replacing compute-hours starting next year) with Amazon reportedly already hedging via model distillation and in-house Nova evaluation — sharpening R&Co's 06-30 "pricing power" flag into a named economic mechanism. AWS is also visibly expanding the moat further into services (R&Co Redburn: $1bn Forward Deployed Engineering unit, Palantir-style, 07-01) — bullish for AWS TAM breadth, bearish for IT-services comps. **07-07: GS (Sheridan) puts a bow on the AWS-acceleration long ahead of the Q2 print — Buy, PT raised $325→$335, AWS growth lifted to ~33%/~35% '26/'27, 2027-30 AWS capex raised (~$827B '26-'28, $50-60B/GW build cost, FCFE negative in '27), Trainium framed as a forward-margin lever, and the consumer read (Prime Day) stable-but-price-sensitive — the most complete single-note statement of the four investor debates (consumer / capex-ROIC / AWS growth / custom silicon) the desk flow has been circling all quarter.** **07-08: BofA (Fenske) puts AMZN on the TMT ideas-call Long list (with GOOGL, the semicaps, and TXN/ADI) — desk positioning turning constructive into the print even as the stock de-rates; Susquehanna (Patil) hosting a Jun/Jul "Search and YouTube Trends" expert call flagged for GOOGL/AMZN keeps the ad/search-demand read-through live ahead of Q2.** **07-11/12: the cross-hyperscaler capex notes both land — Mizuho pegs hyperscaler-only capex at $846B/$912B '27/'28 (up 54%/66% off YE-25 cons) with sector FCF dipping to $68B/$61B '26/'27E before inflecting in '28, and MS lifts AMZN company-wide capex to $308bn/$318bn while modeling AWS with the most available compute of any hyperscaler by '28 (~35 GW) — both reinforce the AWS-capacity-leadership + capex-up narrative into the print.** **07-12: BofA's buyside bogey poll (Fenske) shows the reaccel long has crossed to the buyside base case — AWS bogey +34.3% Q2 → +35.7% Q3 (reported), slightly above GS — while the buyside capex bogeys ($204.5bn'26/$277.7bn'27, ramping to $306.6bn'28/$326.1bn'29) sit below the top-end company-wide sell-side capex (MS $308bn'27, different basis), i.e. the buyside is underwriting the AWS growth without yet marking the most aggressive capex; buyside Q2/Q3 GAAP EBIT $24.7bn/$25.6bn and 2026/27 GAAP EBIT $108.2bn/$141.0bn.** **07-17/07-20 (into the print): Barclays (Sandler) reiterates OW/$330 with the cleanest statement of the margin-agnostic-hyperscaler thesis — Bedrock earns ~35% OI margin whether it serves Anthropic Sonnet 5 or open-source Kimi 2.5, so token fragmentation doesn't dent AWS margin — while Rothschild/Redburn (Haissl/Han) opens the first hard margin short of the quarter (Neutral, Street-low PT $230: AWS gross margin down 520bp Q4-24→Q1-26, Gen-AI-only book margin ~12%, "strongest margins on thinnest disclosure"). On capital, JPM's joint equity-&-credit call quantifies the funding turn (+42% capex to ~$300bn '27, $92bn raised YTD across 4 currencies, the $25bn Jul-7 issuance widening its own bonds ~20bp, an S&P downgrade trigger at 1.5x net leverage) while keeping AMZN the equity top pick; BofA's Big-Tech-setups round frames the print at 2Q rev ~$198bn / AWS 33-34% (Yipit 35-36%), Anthropic >$2bn of Amazon revenue this quarter and Trainium backlog +$100bn q/q.** **07-22 (BofA 2Q preview into the 07-30 print):** Post reiterates Buy/$310 and models a beat ($198.8bn/$24.1bn vs Street $196.8bn/$23.6bn), RAISES BofA's own AWS growth est to 33% (from 31%) with 3Q accelerating to 36% y/y, flags a possible 2026 capex-outlook raise to $210bn (and '27 to $282bn / $230bn AWS) on memory, and carries $100bn of Anthropic backlog in 2Q — the dedicated preview restating the AWS-reaccel long into the print. **07-26 (read-through, UBS MSFT pre-print note — the last independent demand check before the 7/30 print):** UBS's ~dozen customer/partner conversations describe **"Azure/AWS demand… very healthy"** with cloud-infra checks "very strong over the last several quarters (and indeed this has shown up in the sequential dollar adds of AWS, Azure, and Google Cloud)," hedged as "still healthy albeit with some pockets of caution" — and UBS tells clients outright to trade AWS off the MSFT 7/29 call ("including as a read to AWS"). Two items are net-new to this page. First, a fourth independent house now states the **front-end monetization mechanic** that underwrites the Bedrock margin story: AWS, Azure and Google Cloud "**recognize revenues**" from selling access to OpenAI/Anthropic models and their **1P products "such as Claude Code"** — "a source of revenue upside that goes well beyond serving as providers of compute infrastructure" — with the symmetric warning that open-model routing "could impact the growth of these revenue streams in 2H," and one AWS-specific offset (customers funding token overruns by "optimizing elsewhere in their Azure/AWS spend"). Second, and it lands squarely on the quarter's live margin fight: **UBS contests Redburn's Street-low $230 premise directly** — "we've made the same observation about the margin performance at **AWS, Oracle** and others — on the surface, **AI capex/revs are not landing at the abysmal margin levels that the bears feared**," evidenced on MSFT by gross margin falling only 110bps y/y on $37bn (11% of revs) of AI revenue. Both sides are now on the page unresolved. Field colour: one customer runs **~80% AWS / rest Azure with cloud spend +25% y/y, the marquee driver being SAP-to-RISE "in AWS"**; the counter-anecdote is a customer cutting Azure 5-10% and going to **Google**, not AWS. Positioning marks from the same note's peer table (7/24/26): **AMZN +1% YTD is the best performer of the twelve names (mean -33%) and carries the set's highest non-GAAP P/E at 23.9x/21.4x CY26E/CY27E on +15.0%/+13.4% revenue growth** — i.e. into the print AMZN is the consensus AI-infra long being paid for, not the value option.

MS is modelling a *further* acceleration into 2027, not a plateau at the exit rate. **(2) Back-solving MS's own multiples (~29x '27 / ~22x '28 on a $330 PT) gives ~$11.4 of '27 EPS and ~$15.0 of '28 — well above JPM's $10.38/$12.95 and GS's $10.15/$12.84.** So MS reaches the same $330 as JPM on a *lower* multiple and *higher* earnings: same price target, materially different bet. Also added: bull **$400** (~27x avg $15) / bear **$210** (~17x avg $12); **spot $232.11**, mean consensus PT **$309.55** (range $207-$370), **94% OW / 6% EW**; and the positioning number worth carrying into the print — **options-implied P(>$330) ~11.3% vs P(<$210) ~39.7%, the bear case at roughly 4x the base**, which is a hard counterweight to the "consensus long, sentiment 8/10, most crowded MAG7 name" positioning already logged (07-26). The share decline is entirely a denominator effect, not an allocation cut**, and AWS stays the #3 CoWoS customer behind NVDA (50.0% / 945,000 wafers) and AVGO (22.3% / 421,800), ahead of AMD (7.0%) and MSFT (1.0%, entering the mix for the first time). Directionally consistent with the UBS Trainium3 unit ramp already on the page (1.8mn '26E / 2.8mn '27E).
